However, the **significance** lies in its consolidation of a rapidly evolving research area. By categorizing models and identifying key trends, the survey provides a valuable resource for navigating the complex landscape of Transformer-based EEG decoding. The discussion of challenges and future directions helps to highlight important areas for further investigation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of Transformer-based approaches to EEG decoding.
*   **Clear Categorization:** The division of models into direct applications, hybrid architectures, and customized Transformers provides a logical framework for understanding the field.
*   **Identification of Key Trends:** The paper highlights important trends in EEG processing using Transformers.
*   **Discussion of Challenges and Future Directions:** This section offers valuable insights for future research efforts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The paper lacks original analyses or insights, primarily serving as a synthesis of existing research.
*   **Descriptive Nature:** The survey primarily describes existing methods without in-depth critical comparisons or evaluations.

**Potential Influence on the Field:**

The survey has the potential to guide researchers by providing a clear overview of the current state of Transformer applications in EEG decoding. The identification of challenges and future directions may help to focus future research efforts on important areas. By providing links to datasets and source code, it promotes collaboration and progress.

Score: 6

**Rationale:** The score of 6 reflects the paper's limited novelty, as it primarily synthesizes existing research. However, the paper's comprehensive coverage, clear categorization, and identification of key trends contribute to its significance and potential usefulness for researchers in the field. The paper is a valuable resource, but it doesn't present transformative new concepts that would warrant a higher score. The survey's main significance is providing a structured view into an emerging field and identifying areas needing further study, but it is not a groundbreaking work itself.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Heeding the Inner Voice: Aligning ControlNet Training via Intermediate Features Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02321v1)
- **Authors**: Nina Konovalova, Maxim Nikolaev, Andrey Kuznetsov, Aibek Alanov
- **Abstract**: Despite significant progress in text-to-image diffusion models, achieving precise spatial control over generated outputs remains challenging. ControlNet addresses this by introducing an auxiliary conditioning module, while ControlNet++ further refines alignment through a cycle consistency loss applied only to the final denoising steps. However, this approach neglects intermediate generation stages, limiting its effectiveness. We propose InnerControl, a training strategy that enforces spatial consistency across all diffusion steps. Our method trains lightweight convolutional probes to reconstruct input control signals (e.g., edges, depth) from intermediate UNet features at every denoising step. These probes efficiently extract signals even from highly noisy latents, enabling pseudo ground truth controls for training. By minimizing the discrepancy between predicted and target conditions throughout the entire diffusion process, our alignment loss improves both control fidelity and generation quality. Combined with established techniques like ControlNet++, InnerControl achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse conditioning methods (e.g., edges, depth).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces InnerControl, a novel training strategy to improve the spatial control alignment in text-to-image diffusion models, particularly within the ControlNet framework. InnerControl addresses the limitations of methods like ControlNet++, which primarily focus on aligning control signals only during the later denoising steps. InnerControl enforces consistency throughout the entire diffusion process by training small convolutional networks (control prediction probes) to reconstruct input control signals (e.g., edges, depth) from intermediate UNet features at each denoising step.  This allows for an alignment loss to be calculated between the predicted and target condition at every stage, resulting in improved control fidelity and image quality. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of InnerControl in different condition methods such as edge and depth.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of enforcing alignment *across all* diffusion steps, rather than focusing solely on the late stages, is a significant contribution. Prior work acknowledged the importance of early diffusion stages in determining structure, but extending reward losses to earlier steps resulted in artifacts.  InnerControl overcomes this limitation by using lightweight probe networks to extract control signals effectively even from noisy early-stage latents. This allows for a more holistic approach to control alignment. The integration of these lightweight networks represents a clever solution to a significant problem. The use of lightweight convolutional networks inspired by Readout Guidance is not entirely novel in itself, but *applying* this idea within the context of ControlNet and diffusion models to achieve consistent spatial control throughout denoising is the key innovation.

*   **Significance:** The improvement in control alignment, as evidenced by the reduced RMSE in depth estimation and increased SSIM in edge detection, is impactful. The experimental results demonstrate clear benefits over existing methods like ControlNet++ and Ctrl-U, especially at higher guidance scales where control fidelity is crucial. The qualitative results also visually corroborate these improvements, showing reduced artifacts and better adherence to the input control signals. The results suggest that InnerControl addresses a key bottleneck in controllable image generation and opens avenues for future research focusing on more robust and accurate control signal extraction throughout the denoising trajectory.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined solution.
    *   Rigorous experimental evaluation with quantitative metrics (RMSE, SSIM, FID, CLIP score) and qualitative analysis.
    *   Demonstrated improvements across multiple control signal types (depth, edges).
    *   Ablation studies that justify design choices (e.g., the use of 920 steps for alignment loss).
    *   The paper is easy to follow and well-written.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the results are promising, the improvement in FID isn't always consistently significant, suggesting that perceptual quality gains are marginal in some cases. More detailed analysis of FID vs other metrics would be helpful.
    *   The computational overhead of training the additional lightweight networks is not discussed in detail. This could be a practical limitation in some scenarios.
    *   The paper doesn't explore the limits of how "early" the control probes can be applied and still be effective. Pushing this boundary could lead to further insights.
    *   The authors acknowledge that their implementation makes use of an existing codebase. While a practical choice, this reduces the direct impact on the field.

*   **Potential Impact:** InnerControl has the potential to influence future research directions in controllable image generation, particularly in the design of more effective training strategies and architectures for aligning control signals throughout the diffusion process. It also provides a practical approach for improving the control fidelity of existing ControlNet-based systems.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the approach, demonstrated improvements in control alignment, clear experimental validation, and the potential for future research impact, but also considering the marginal FID improvements, the limited analysis of computational overhead, and the reliance on an existing codebase, a score of 7 is assigned.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Offline Reinforcement Learning with Penalized Action Noise Injection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02356v1)
- **Authors**: JunHyeok Oh, Byung-Jun Lee
- **Abstract**: Offline reinforcement learning (RL) optimizes a policy using only a fixed dataset, making it a practical approach in scenarios where interaction with the environment is costly. Due to this limitation, generalization ability is key to improving the performance of offline RL algorithms, as demonstrated by recent successes of offline RL with diffusion models. However, it remains questionable whether such diffusion models are necessary for highly performing offline RL algorithms, given their significant computational requirements during inference. In this paper, we propose Penalized Action Noise Injection (PANI), a method that simply enhances offline learning by utilizing noise-injected actions to cover the entire action space, while penalizing according to the amount of noise injected. This approach is inspired by how diffusion models have worked in offline RL algorithms. We provide a theoretical foundation for this method, showing that offline RL algorithms with such noise-injected actions solve a modified Markov Decision Process (MDP), which we call the noisy action MDP. PANI is compatible with a wide range of existing off-policy and offline RL algorithms, and despite its simplicity, it demonstrates significant performance improvements across various benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Penalized Action Noise Injection (PANI), a method for offline reinforcement learning (RL) that enhances policy learning by injecting noise into actions within the offline dataset.  The method penalizes actions based on the magnitude of the injected noise, which helps to mitigate the overestimation of out-of-distribution (OOD) actions, a key challenge in offline RL.  The authors provide a theoretical justification by formalizing PANI as Q-learning within a Noisy Action Markov Decision Process (NAMDP). They also propose a hybrid noise distribution to improve robustness. The paper demonstrates significant empirical performance gains on standard offline RL benchmarks compared to several baselines, including diffusion-based methods, while maintaining computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of injecting noise and penalizing based on the noise magnitude, while inspired by diffusion models, is relatively novel in its application to mitigating OOD action overestimation in offline RL. The paper's novelty primarily stems from its simplicity and effectiveness in achieving state-of-the-art or near state-of-the-art results compared to more complex diffusion-based approaches. Formalizing the noise injection as a modified MDP (NAMDP) adds to the novelty, although the theoretical results are relatively straightforward. However, the concept of using noise for exploration in RL is not new, and the paper builds on existing ideas in score matching and diffusion models, albeit with a specific focus on offline RL and a computationally efficient implementation. The Hybrid Noise Distribution also presents a unique contribution that balances exploration and robustness.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its practical impact. PANI provides a simple, computationally efficient alternative to complex diffusion-based offline RL algorithms. It achieves significant performance improvements over strong baselines with minimal implementation overhead. The method's broad compatibility with existing off-policy and offline RL algorithms makes it easily adoptable. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating consistent gains across diverse benchmarks. The method's conceptual clarity and ease of implementation could significantly impact practitioners in the field by enabling better performance on offline RL tasks without the heavy computational burden of generative models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Simplicity and Efficiency:** PANI is conceptually simple and easy to implement, making it accessible to practitioners. It is also computationally efficient compared to diffusion-based alternatives.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides a theoretical grounding for the method, formalizing PANI as Q-learning within a Noisy Action MDP (NAMDP), although it is mathematically lightweight.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** PANI demonstrates significant and consistent performance improvements over strong baselines across diverse D4RL benchmarks.
    *   **Broad Compatibility:** PANI is compatible with a wide range of existing off-policy and offline RL algorithms.
    * Hybrid Noise Distribution - The proposed hybrid noise distribution demonstrated better robustness and performance across diverse environments.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** While the NAMDP formulation provides some theoretical justification, the mathematical analysis could be more rigorous and insightful. The theoretical error bounds are pretty standard.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The method's performance is still sensitive to the choice of noise distribution and noise scale.  While the paper suggests guidelines, optimal parameter selection without environment interaction remains a challenge.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While PANI demonstrates significant gains, its core idea is built on established techniques. It's more of an engineering breakthrough rather than a fundamentally new conceptual one.
    *  **Missing Direct Ablations** The paper would be greatly strengthened by a more thorough analysis directly ablating the noise injection against the penalization term to provide a more nuanced understanding of which components contribute most to the performance.

*   **Potential Impact:** PANI has the potential to become a widely adopted technique in offline RL due to its simplicity, efficiency, and effectiveness. It could enable better policy learning from limited offline data in real-world applications.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7/10**.

Here's the rationale: PANI demonstrates significant practical value by offering a computationally efficient alternative to complex diffusion-based methods in offline RL, achieving strong empirical performance. It also provides a good enough theoretical rationale. However, its core ideas are rooted in existing techniques, and the theoretical analysis lacks significant depth. Also, there needs to be better ablations to justify design choices. Nevertheless, its simplicity, effectiveness, broad applicability, and potential to make offline RL more accessible to practitioners justify a solid score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Coling-UniA at SciVQA 2025: Few-Shot Example Retrieval and Confidence-Informed Ensembling for Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02357v1)
- **Authors**: Christian Jaumann, Annemarie Friedrich, Rainer Lienhart
- **Abstract**: This paper describes our system for the SciVQA 2025 Shared Task on Scientific Visual Question Answering. Our system employs an ensemble of two Multimodal Large Language Models and various few-shot example retrieval strategies. The model and few-shot setting are selected based on the figure and question type. We also select answers based on the models' confidence levels. On the blind test data, our system ranks third out of seven with an average F1 score of 85.12 across ROUGE-1, ROUGE-L, and BERTS. Our code is publicly available.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a system for the SciVQA 2025 Shared Task, which focuses on scientific visual question answering. The system utilizes an ensemble of two multimodal large language models (MLLMs) in zero/few-shot settings. The selection of models and few-shot examples is based on figure and question types, and answers are chosen based on model confidence levels. The authors explore different strategies for few-shot example retrieval, including question similarity and question-image similarity. The paper reports results on the development and test sets, highlighting the effectiveness of their ensembling and confidence-informed answer selection strategies. The system ranked third in the official evaluation with an average F1 score of 85.12.

**Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem in VQA, specifically in the scientific domain.
    *   The use of MLLMs in zero/few-shot settings is a practical approach that avoids the need for fine-tuning, which can be computationally expensive.
    *   The exploration of different few-shot example retrieval strategies provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of various similarity measures.
    *   The ensembling and confidence-informed answer selection strategies are well-motivated and shown to improve performance.
    *   The paper includes ablation studies and analysis of results by question and figure type, which provides a deeper understanding of the system's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   Open-weight models ensure reproducibility.
    *   Detailed analysis by prompt template, question, and figure type offers valuable insights.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty of the approach is somewhat limited. While ensembling and confidence-based selection are useful techniques, they are not entirely novel in the context of VQA.
    *   The improvement over simpler baselines may not be substantial enough to warrant a high novelty score. The paper could benefit from a more thorough comparison with existing state-of-the-art VQA methods for charts, even if they are fine-tuned.
    *   The paper acknowledges that some of the images may have already been seen by the MLLMs during training, which could inflate the reported results.
    *   The authors also recognize limitations in the diversity and realism of the unanswerable questions.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on a single metric (ROUGE-1 F1), with less emphasis on other relevant metrics.
    *   The dependence on image metadata for prompting introduces some complexity and may limit generalizability.

*   **Significance:**

The paper offers practical insights into applying MLLMs to scientific VQA in a few-shot setting. The results demonstrate that even without fine-tuning, MLLMs can achieve reasonable performance, and that careful ensembling and confidence-based selection can further improve results. While not a groundbreaking advancement, it contributes useful practical knowledge to the field. It demonstrates that a zero/few-shot approach is a viable solution for SciVQA.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid engineering approach to the SciVQA task, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining existing techniques such as ensembling and confidence-informed selection with MLLMs in a few-shot setting. However, the limited novelty of the individual components, the potential for data leakage due to pre-training of the MLLMs on similar data, and the dependence on metadata slightly reduce the overall impact. The paper contributes useful insights but does not represent a significant paradigm shift in the field. The third-place rank in the shared task supports the validity of the method, however, it also represents that there are systems that perform better. Therefore, a score of 6 is appropriate, reflecting a worthwhile but not exceptional contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Holistic Tokenizer for Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02358v1)
- **Authors**: Anlin Zheng, Haochen Wang, Yucheng Zhao, Weipeng Deng, Tiancai Wang, Xiangyu Zhang, Xiaojuan Qi
- **Abstract**: The vanilla autoregressive image generation model generates visual tokens in a step-by-step fashion, which limits the ability to capture holistic relationships among token sequences. Moreover, most visual tokenizers map local image patches into latent tokens, leading to limited global information. To address this, we introduce \textit{Hita}, a novel image tokenizer for autoregressive (AR) image generation. It introduces a holistic-to-local tokenization scheme with learnable holistic queries and local patch tokens. Besides, Hita incorporates two key strategies for improved alignment with the AR generation process: 1) it arranges a sequential structure with holistic tokens at the beginning followed by patch-level tokens while using causal attention to maintain awareness of previous tokens; and 2) before feeding the de-quantized tokens into the decoder, Hita adopts a lightweight fusion module to control information flow to prioritize holistic tokens. Extensive experiments show that Hita accelerates the training speed of AR generators and outperforms those trained with vanilla tokenizers, achieving \textbf{2.59 FID} and \textbf{281.9 IS} on the ImageNet benchmark. A detailed analysis of the holistic representation highlights its ability to capture global image properties such as textures, materials, and shapes. Additionally, Hita also demonstrates effectiveness in zero-shot style transfer and image in-painting. The code is available at \href{https://github.com/CVMI-Lab/Hita}{https://github.com/CVMI-Lab/Hita}
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Holistic Tokenizer for Autoregressive Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hita, a novel image tokenizer designed specifically for autoregressive (AR) image generation.  Hita addresses limitations of existing tokenizers that often rely on local patch-level representations, which hinders the ability to capture global image properties and leads to coherence issues in AR generation. Hita employs a "holistic-to-local" tokenization scheme, using learnable queries to extract global features and then fusing them with local patch tokens. The design includes a sequential structure for token generation (holistic tokens first) and a fusion module to prioritize holistic information during decoding. Experiments demonstrate that Hita accelerates training, improves image quality (FID, IS) on ImageNet, and enables zero-shot style transfer and in-painting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of a "holistic-to-local" tokenization is a valuable contribution. The design aspects of Hita, particularly the learnable queries, the sequential generation structure, and the fusion module for prioritizing holistic information, demonstrate a clear effort to improve AR image generation by addressing its inherent limitations regarding global coherence.
*   **Significance:** The claimed results are compelling, showing notable improvements in FID and IS scores compared to existing AR models like LlamaGen and outperforming some diffusion models (LDM). The demonstration of zero-shot style transfer and image in-painting adds practical value and highlights the benefits of the holistic representation. The speedup in training time is also significant.

However, the paper has the following weaknesses:

*   **Limited novelty regarding pre-trained embeddings:** While incorporating pre-trained foundation models for injecting semantics is a standard practice, the way Hita does it is not sufficiently distinct. DINOv2 is a well-established approach, and the contribution is incremental.
*   **The claim to beat existing diffusion models needs careful consideration.**  LDM-4, cited in the paper, is an older diffusion model. The paper needs to include results comparing Hita with more recent and advanced diffusion models like SDXL and others.

*   **Limited Discussion of failure modes:** The paper focuses predominantly on successes. Discussion on failure modes, such as limitations of the in-painting or style transfer or when the holistic tokens fail to adequately capture global information, would strengthen the analysis.
*   **Evaluation limited to a few tasks:** While ImageNet and demonstration of zero-shot applications are compelling, the scope of evaluation can be expanded to include other relevant tasks such as controllable generation.
*   **Clarity of writing can be improved:** The paper contains a few writing issues that somewhat hinder readability, especially in the ablation studies section.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable idea (holistic-to-local tokenization) and achieves notable improvements in the performance of AR image generation.  The approach is demonstrated on a standard benchmark (ImageNet). However, the novelty is somewhat limited by the incremental nature of incorporating a pre-trained foundation model, and the results need to be compared to more recent diffusion models. While results demonstrate improved semantic consistency, the discussions regarding the method need further justification regarding the loss functions and better visuals. Furthermore, in-depth explanations and comparisons regarding other possible token fusion methods would strenghten the manuscript.

Therefore, I give the paper a score of:

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### QFFN-BERT: An Empirical Study of Depth, Performance, and Data Efficiency in Hybrid Quantum-Classical Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02364v1)
- **Authors**: Pilsung Kang
- **Abstract**: Parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) have recently emerged as promising components for enhancing the expressibility of neural architectures. In this work, we introduce QFFN-BERT, a hybrid quantum-classical transformer where the feedforward network (FFN) modules of a compact BERT variant are replaced by PQC-based layers. This design is motivated by the dominant parameter contribution of FFNs, which account for approximately two-thirds of the parameters within standard Transformer encoder blocks. While prior studies have primarily integrated PQCs into self-attention modules, our work focuses on the FFN and systematically investigates the trade-offs between PQC depth, expressibility, and trainability. Our final PQC architecture incorporates a residual connection, both $R_Y$ and $R_Z$ rotations, and an alternating entanglement strategy to ensure stable training and high expressibility. Our experiments, conducted on a classical simulator, on the SST-2 and DBpedia benchmarks demonstrate two key findings. First, a carefully configured QFFN-BERT achieves up to 102.0% of the baseline accuracy, surpassing its classical counterpart in a full-data setting while reducing FFN-specific parameters by over 99%. Second, our model exhibits a consistent and competitive edge in few-shot learning scenarios, confirming its potential for superior data efficiency. These results, supported by an ablation study on a non-optimized PQC that failed to learn, confirm that PQCs can serve as powerful and parameter-efficient alternatives to classical FFNs when co-designed with foundational deep learning principles.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the QFFN-BERT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QFFN-BERT, a hybrid quantum-classical transformer model where the feedforward network (FFN) modules in a compact BERT variant are replaced with parameterized quantum circuit (PQC) layers. The authors motivate this design by the fact that FFNs contribute significantly to the total parameters in standard transformer models. They systematically explore the trade-offs between PQC depth, expressibility, and trainability. Experiments on SST-2 and DBpedia datasets show that a carefully configured QFFN-BERT can achieve competitive or even superior accuracy compared to its classical counterpart while significantly reducing FFN-specific parameters. The model also exhibits competitive performance in few-shot learning scenarios, suggesting improved data efficiency.  The study includes an ablation study demonstrating the importance of co-designing the quantum and classical components, emphasizing the role of residual connections and PQC designs that enhance trainability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty in Architecture:** The core idea of replacing FFN layers with PQCs in a Transformer is a valuable exploration. While quantum self-attention has received more attention, targeting FFNs is a structurally sound and potentially parameter-efficient approach, given the FFN's local, position-wise operation. This represents a somewhat novel component within the broad area of Quantum Machine Learning applied to NLP.
*   **Systematic Exploration:** The paper's strength lies in its methodical exploration of PQC depth. Varying the depth and observing the impact on performance, generalization, and trainability provides valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities of hybrid quantum-classical models.
*   **Data Efficiency:** The demonstrated improvement in few-shot learning is a significant finding. The potential for quantum models to achieve better data efficiency is a key motivator for this research area, and the results support this potential.
*   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study with the "vanilla" PQC is crucial. It reinforces the need for careful co-design and demonstrates that simply incorporating a quantum circuit without proper architectural considerations (residual connections, appropriate rotations, entanglement strategies) can lead to poor performance.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide details about their setup (hardware, software, hyperparameters), which enhances reproducibility.
*   **Clearly Defined Scope:** The paper is upfront about the limitations of current NISQ hardware and focuses on simulated experiments to explore architectural trade-offs. This realistic framing is important.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scale and Realism:**  The experiments are conducted using a classical simulator, which significantly limits the scale and does not address the specific challenges of running these circuits on actual quantum hardware (decoherence, gate errors, etc.). The bert-tiny model, while useful for exploration, is not representative of modern, large-scale transformers.
*   **Modest Performance Gains:** While the QFFN-BERT achieves competitive accuracy, the gains over the baseline are relatively small. Given the computational overhead of simulating quantum circuits, the practical benefits of the current design are limited. The percentage differences (e.g. increase from 79.59 to 81.19 on SST2) are not dramatically significant.
*   **Computational Cost:**  The CPU-only simulation of PQCs in Qiskit poses a major bottleneck.  The paper acknowledges the runtime issues but could have provided more details in describing how this was mitigated (e.g. batch size, optimizations, etc.). The inability to complete an 8-layer model DBpedia evaluation highlights the problem.
*   **Limited Novelty in PQC Design:** While the architectural integration is novel, the PQC designs themselves (Ry/Rz rotations, CNOT/CZ entanglement) are relatively standard. There is not a deep exploration or justification of why these particular choices were made relative to other PQC architectures.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** There's a limited theoretical understanding of why the proposed PQC architecture works or how it relates to the underlying NLP task. While the paper points to the barren plateau phenomenon, it does not provide any in-depth analysis or theoretical justification.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its exploration of a novel hybrid quantum-classical architecture and its systematic analysis of PQC depth and co-design principles. While the performance gains are modest, the work identifies key challenges and opportunities for integrating quantum circuits into Transformers. The findings related to data efficiency and the importance of residual connections are valuable contributions to the field. The analysis will motivate other research into efficient hybrid QML approaches for NLP.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6** to this paper.

**Rationale:**

While the paper demonstrates some promising results, the limited scale, modest performance gains, and lack of theoretical depth prevent it from being a truly transformative contribution. The use of a classical simulator and a small bert-tiny model means the results are difficult to directly extrapolate to real-world NLP applications on quantum hardware. However, the systematic exploration of PQC depth, the demonstration of data efficiency, and the validation of co-design principles are valuable findings. The architecture is novel, and the results suggest interesting directions for future research. The score acknowledges the paper's valuable insights and potential for future development but also reflects the limitations of the current work. The paper provides a valuable, well-executed initial study on using quantum circuits in FFN modules, but further work is needed for the proposed ideas to translate into impactful applications.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### UVLM: Benchmarking Video Language Model for Underwater World Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02373v1)
- **Authors**: Xizhe Xue, Yang Zhou, Dawei Yan, Ying Li, Haokui Zhang, Rong Xiao
- **Abstract**: Recently, the remarkable success of large language models (LLMs) has achieved a profound impact on the field of artificial intelligence. Numerous advanced works based on LLMs have been proposed and applied in various scenarios. Among them, video language models (VidLMs) are particularly widely used. However, existing works primarily focus on terrestrial scenarios, overlooking the highly demanding application needs of underwater observation. To overcome this gap, we introduce UVLM, an under water observation benchmark which is build through a collaborative approach combining human expertise and AI models. To ensure data quality, we have conducted in-depth considerations from multiple perspectives. First, to address the unique challenges of underwater environments, we selected videos that represent typical underwater challenges including light variations, water turbidity, and diverse viewing angles to construct the dataset. Second, to ensure data diversity, the dataset covers a wide range of frame rates, resolutions, 419 classes of marine animals, and various static plants and terrains. Next, for task diversity, we adopted a structured design where observation targets are categorized into two major classes: biological and environmental. Each category includes content observation and change/action observation, totaling 20 distinct task types. Finally, we designed several challenging evaluation metrics to enable quantitative comparison and analysis of different methods. Experiments on two representative VidLMs demonstrate that fine-tuning VidLMs on UVLM significantly improves underwater world understanding while also showing potential for slight improvements on existing in-air VidLM benchmarks, such as VideoMME and Perception text. The dataset and prompt engineering will be released publicly.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces UVLM, a new benchmark dataset for video language models (VidLMs) specifically designed for underwater environments.  The authors motivate this work by highlighting the limitations of existing VidLMs, which primarily focus on terrestrial scenarios and struggle with the unique challenges of underwater imagery (e.g., light attenuation, turbidity, lack of domain-specific knowledge).  UVLM is constructed through a collaborative process involving human expertise and AI models, ensuring data quality and diversity.  The dataset includes videos with a wide range of frame rates, resolutions, marine animal classes, and static plants/terrains. It covers 20 distinct task types related to biological and environmental observations.  The authors also design evaluation metrics tailored to underwater video understanding. Experiments on two VidLMs demonstrate the effectiveness of fine-tuning on UVLM, leading to improved performance in underwater tasks and, potentially, slight gains on some existing in-air benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the creation of a specialized benchmark for underwater video language understanding.  While other datasets exist for underwater image or video analysis, this is the first comprehensive effort to combine both video and natural language for this domain. This fills a clear gap in the current VidLM landscape. The structured design of the dataset with specific tasks and the use of human/AI collaboration for annotation are also valuable contributions.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a real-world need for underwater observation and analysis. Current VidLMs cannot perform well in underwater environment, and UVLM provides a resource to train and evaluate models designed for this purpose. The inclusion of domain-specific knowledge and a variety of task types increases the potential impact for marine biology and related fields. Improvement on underwater tasks could lead to improved understanding of aquatic ecosystems and faster automation of a variety of important real-world tasks.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with applying existing VidLMs to underwater scenarios.
    * **Comprehensive Dataset Design:**  The construction process appears well-thought-out, with attention paid to data quality, diversity, and task relevance.
    * **Evaluation Metrics:** The development of specialized evaluation metrics demonstrates a thorough understanding of the specific challenges of underwater video language understanding.
    * **Experimental Results:** The experiments provide evidence that UVLM can be used to improve the performance of existing VidLMs on underwater tasks.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on GPT-4:** Reliance on a closed source model (GPT-4) for annotation, could raise concerns about reproducibility and transparency. While manual correction helps, the underlying data may be biased towards GPT-4's biases or limited knowledge. A fully open-source annotation pipeline would have been ideal.
    * **Limited Baseline Models:** Only two base VidLMs were fine-tuned and evaluated. Evaluation on a wider range of architectures would strengthen the claim of general applicability.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by creating a dedicated benchmark for underwater video language understanding. The dataset fills a gap in the VidLM landscape and facilitates the development of models that can address the specific challenges of this domain.  While there are some limitations, specifically the reliance on a closed-source model (GPT-4) in the annotation pipeline and limited baseline models, the overall design, quality, and potential impact of UVLM warrant a positive assessment.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Code LLM Training via Distribution-Consistent and Diversity-Aware Data Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02378v1)
- **Authors**: Weijie Lyu, Sheng-Jun Huang, Xuan Xia
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have significantly improved code generation and program comprehension, accelerating the evolution of software engineering. Current methods primarily enhance model performance by leveraging vast amounts of data, focusing on data quantity while often overlooking data quality, thereby reducing training efficiency. To address this, we introduce an approach that utilizes a parametric model for code data selection, aimed at improving both training efficiency and model performance. Our method optimizes the parametric model to ensure distribution consistency and diversity within the selected subset, guaranteeing high-quality data. Experimental results demonstrate that using only 10K samples, our method achieves gains of 2.4% (HumanEval) and 2.3% (MBPP) over 92K full-sampled baseline, outperforming other sampling approaches in both performance and efficiency. This underscores that our method effectively boosts model performance while significantly reducing computational costs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel approach to efficient code LLM training by using a parametric model for data selection.  Instead of focusing on the sheer quantity of training data, the authors emphasize data quality by selecting a subset of the original dataset that preserves distribution consistency and maximizes diversity. They map data samples to a high-dimensional feature space and optimize a parametric model within that space to balance these two objectives. The optimized model is then used to select a high-quality subset of data for fine-tuning a code LLM.  Experiments with DeepSeek-Coder-Base-6.7B using a 92K Python dataset show that their method, using only 10K sampled data, achieves better performance than training on the entire dataset and outperforms other sampling techniques in both performance and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in introducing a parametric model into the *code-specific* data selection process. While parametric models have been used in active learning and data selection contexts before (as acknowledged by the authors referring to ActiveFT), their application to code and the specific focus on balancing distribution consistency and diversity in the *feature space* represents a significant contribution. Traditional approaches to code data selection often rely on discrete methods or external oracles, which can be computationally expensive. The feature-space approach allows for efficient and continuous optimization.
    The idea of prioritizing data quality over quantity is not new, but the specific implementation tailored for code using a parametric model with distribution and diversity constraints makes this paper a unique contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to drastically reduce the computational costs associated with training high-performing code LLMs. By demonstrating that a carefully selected subset of data can outperform training on the entire dataset, the authors pave the way for more efficient model development. This is particularly relevant given the increasing size and complexity of LLMs. The results showing that a smaller subset significantly outperforms a full dataset is a powerful argument for more thoughtful data curation techniques. This has potential benefits for resource-constrained researchers or those looking to rapidly prototype or customize code LLMs for specific tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-defined methodology:** The paper presents a clear and easy-to-follow methodology with a well-defined optimization objective.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method, showcasing performance gains over baseline approaches and highlighting the efficiency of the technique.
    *   **Focus on practical considerations:** The paper addresses the practical challenges of training large models, emphasizing both performance and efficiency.
    *   **Code Availability:** Making code available makes experiments reproducible.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope:** The experimental validation is primarily limited to the Python programming language and a single base model (DeepSeekCoder-Base-6.7B). While this is understandable given resource constraints, it limits the generalizability of the findings. The performance may vary when adapted to other code models or to different programming languages.
    *   **Specific datasets:** Experiments should've been designed to test the general applicability.
    *   **Lack of analysis of selected data:** The paper focuses primarily on performance metrics but provides limited analysis of the specific characteristics of the selected data subset.  Understanding *why* the selected data is more effective would provide additional insights. More analysis around why the 10K performs significantly better is desirable.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient code LLM training strategies. The proposed method could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners looking to reduce computational costs and improve model performance through data selection. The emphasis on distribution consistency and diversity could also inspire new data curation techniques in other domains.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of code LLM training by introducing a novel and efficient data selection strategy. While there are limitations regarding the scope of the experimental validation, the strong empirical results and clear methodology make this a significant contribution. The focus on reducing computational costs and promoting efficiency is highly relevant in the current landscape of large language models. The implementation details and comparison to the SOTA are thorough.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### JoyTTS: LLM-based Spoken Chatbot With Voice Cloning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02380v1)
- **Authors**: Fangru Zhou, Jun Zhao, Guoxin Wang
- **Abstract**: JoyTTS is an end-to-end spoken chatbot that combines large language models (LLM) with text-to-speech (TTS) technology, featuring voice cloning capabilities. This project is built upon the open-source MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2 models and trained on 2000 hours of conversational data. We have also provided the complete training code to facilitate further development and optimization by the community. On the testing machine seed-tts-zh, it achieves a SS (speaker similarity) score of 0.73 and a WER (Word Error Rate) of 5.09. The code and models, along with training and inference scripts, are available at https://github.com/jdh-algo/JoyTTS.git.
- **Summary**: ## Summary:

The paper introduces JoyTTS, an end-to-end spoken chatbot leveraging large language models (LLMs) and text-to-speech (TTS) technology with voice cloning capabilities. Built upon the open-source MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2 models and trained on a 2000-hour conversational dataset, JoyTTS aims to improve voice cloning performance. The paper details the model's architecture, including the Tokenizer, LLM-Chat, LLM-TTS, and Generator modules. It also describes the data construction process, the training strategy (independent and joint training of LLM and TTS modules), and the evaluation results using speaker similarity (SS) and word error rate (WER) metrics. The results suggest that JoyTTS produces high-quality, natural-sounding speech with precise voice cloning and impressive computational efficiency. The authors provide the code and models publicly to promote further research and development.

## Critical Evaluation:

**Novelty:** The paper presents an interesting combination of existing open-source models, MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2, to create a spoken chatbot with voice cloning functionality. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their specific integration and the end-to-end approach contribute a degree of novelty.  The addition of using hidden states from the LLM-Chat module to inform the LLM-TTS module shows a thoughtful approach to enhance semantic understanding for the TTS process.  However, the architecture itself (LLM + TTS) is a well-trodden path, which reduces the innovation. The specific data augmentation strategies employed, splitting dialogues and adding punctuation, are also not fundamentally new, though they contribute to improved robustness. The open-sourcing of the code and models is also a helpful contribution to the community.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies primarily in providing a practical and accessible implementation of a spoken chatbot with voice cloning. The performance metrics (SS and WER) demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach, and the low latency makes it suitable for real-time applications. Releasing the complete training code significantly increases its impact, allowing other researchers to replicate and extend the work. However, the lack of comparison to other state-of-the-art end-to-end spoken chatbot systems, particularly those also incorporating voice cloning, is a significant weakness. Without such comparison, it is difficult to assess the true advancement represented by JoyTTS. Additionally, while the latency is reasonable, more extensive benchmarks, particularly relating to resource usage and scalability, would be beneficial to understand practical applicability. The discussion of future work, including emotion control, shows potential for further development and increasing its relevance.

**Justification:** The strength of the paper lies in its practical implementation of a voice-cloning chatbot system leveraging publicly available resources and datasets. The clear architecture description and open-sourcing enable reproducibility and further development.  However, the major weakness is the lack of comparative analysis against alternative systems, reducing confidence that it significantly pushes the state-of-the-art. The novelty is incremental, combining existing models in a useful way rather than presenting a fundamentally new approach. The overall impact is moderated by the limited evaluation and comparison. While the open-sourcing is a positive point, it only increases the relevance contingent to the overall system performance. Without a comprehensive and comparative analysis, it's difficult to see significant impact in the field.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### Evaluating Language Models For Threat Detection in IoT Security Logs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02390v1)
- **Authors**: Jorge J. Tejero-Fernández, Alfonso Sánchez-Macián
- **Abstract**: Log analysis is a relevant research field in cybersecurity as they can provide a source of information for the detection of threats to networks and systems. This paper presents a pipeline to use fine-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs) for anomaly detection and mitigation recommendation using IoT security logs. Utilizing classical machine learning classifiers as a baseline, three open-source LLMs are compared for binary and multiclass anomaly detection, with three strategies: zero-shot, few-shot prompting and fine-tuning using an IoT dataset. LLMs give better results on multi-class attack classification than the corresponding baseline models. By mapping detected threats to MITRE CAPEC, defining a set of IoT-specific mitigation actions, and fine-tuning the models with those actions, the models are able to provide a combined detection and recommendation guidance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of fine-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs) for threat detection and mitigation recommendation in IoT security logs. It compares the performance of traditional machine learning classifiers (Random Forest, XGBoost, MLP, and LightGBM) and open-source LLMs (DeepSeek, LLaMA, and Qwen) using the Edge-IIoTset dataset. The paper evaluates binary (malicious vs. benign) and multi-class (specific attack type) classification tasks under zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuning strategies. Additionally, it proposes a method to integrate mitigation recommendations into the LLM responses by mapping detected threats to MITRE CAPEC and fine-tuning models with IoT-specific mitigation actions. The results show that fine-tuned LLMs outperform traditional ML models in multi-class classification, especially when trained with fewer samples. The DeepSeek model is found to be the most robust and consistent across tasks, and its ability to generate actionable mitigations using a fine-tuned approach is highlighted as a significant contribution.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strengths:** The combination of threat detection and mitigation within a single LLM-based pipeline is a valuable contribution. While individual LLMs have been used for threat detection, the mitigation aspect is less explored. The paper systematically investigates zero-shot, few-shot and fine-tuning strategies, which offers a broader comparison. Adapting the mitigation actions from CAPEC framework specifically for IoT environments is also a nice touch.
    *   **Weaknesses:** The use of LLMs for threat detection in general is not entirely novel. Numerous papers have explored this area. The approach is largely incremental (applying existing techniques to a specific dataset and task). The chosen models (DeepSeek, LLaMA, Qwen) are relatively standard. While there is some experimentation with data-scarce scenarios, the datasets used are established benchmarks. The use of LoRA is also a standard fine tuning approach.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strengths:** Demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs in a resource-constrained IoT setting is significant, particularly if the methods are deployable in real-time (which isn't addressed). The improved accuracy compared to traditional ML models in multi-class classification tasks is a positive result. Providing actionable mitigations addresses a practical need in security operations, potentially reducing manual workload.
    *   **Weaknesses:** The lack of real-time evaluation limits the practical significance. The limited mitigation scope makes it harder to adopt in real-world deployments. The dataset, although relevant, may not represent the full complexity and diversity of real-world IoT environments. The dependence on CAPEC limits the novelty in the recommendation aspect.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper provides a solid experimental evaluation of LLMs for IoT security log analysis, particularly for multi-class attack classification and mitigation generation. However, it builds upon existing research with incremental improvements. The evaluation of the techniques on a specific dataset shows improvements that could be potentially significant.

    **Major Weaknesses:** The study lacks significant novelty in its approach or methodology. It applies existing techniques to a specific problem area, and the gains are incremental. The evaluation metrics for mitigation generation could be more robust and comprehensive. There is a limited discussion on the challenges and practical issues for deploying the proposed solution.

    **Major Strengths:** The paper provides a comprehensive experimental evaluation of LLMs for IoT security logs. It integrates threat detection with mitigation suggestion to provide a more complete security solution.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 reflects the paper's valuable contribution through its experimental evaluation and specific focus on IoT security, offset by its limited novelty and areas for improvement. While the paper does not present a groundbreaking method or significant breakthrough, its systematic approach, evaluation on the IoT domain, and the integration of mitigation recommendations are valuable. A higher score would require a more innovative methodology, a more comprehensive evaluation (especially related to practical deployment considerations), or a more significant advance in the state of the art.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Posterior Transition Modeling for Unsupervised Diffusion-Based Speech Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02391v1)
- **Authors**: Mostafa Sadeghi, Jean-Eudes Ayilo, Romain Serizel, Xavier Alameda-Pineda
- **Abstract**: We explore unsupervised speech enhancement using diffusion models as expressive generative priors for clean speech. Existing approaches guide the reverse diffusion process using noisy speech through an approximate, noise-perturbed likelihood score, combined with the unconditional score via a trade-off hyperparameter. In this work, we propose two alternative algorithms that directly model the conditional reverse transition distribution of diffusion states. The first method integrates the diffusion prior with the observation model in a principled way, removing the need for hyperparameter tuning. The second defines a diffusion process over the noisy speech itself, yielding a fully tractable and exact likelihood score. Experiments on the WSJ0-QUT and VoiceBank-DEMAND datasets demonstrate improved enhancement metrics and greater robustness to domain shifts compared to both supervised and unsupervised baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents DEPSE (Diffusion-based Explicit Posterior Sampling for Speech Enhancement), a new approach to unsupervised speech enhancement using diffusion models. The core idea is to model the conditional (reverse) transition distribution of diffusion states directly, instead of relying on approximate likelihoods and trade-off hyperparameters as in existing unsupervised methods. The authors propose two algorithms: DEPSE-IL, which integrates the diffusion prior and observation model, and DEPSE-TL, which defines a diffusion process directly over the noisy speech signal, leading to a tractable likelihood. The methods are evaluated on WSJ0-QUT and VoiceBank-DEMAND datasets, showing improvements in enhancement metrics and robustness to domain shifts compared to supervised and unsupervised baselines.  DEPSE-TL emerges as the superior of the two variants, excelling in metrics such as SI-SDR, PESQ and ESTOI compared to the prior methods UDiffSE+ and DEPSE-IL. Crucially, DEPSE-TL exhibits greater resilience to performance degradation when encountering domain mismatches between training and testing data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant improvement over existing unsupervised diffusion-based SE methods. Modeling the conditional transition distribution explicitly is a novel approach. DEPSE-TL, in particular, is innovative in defining a diffusion process over the noisy speech directly, enabling exact likelihood computation and improving performance and robustness. While the overall concept of using diffusion models for speech enhancement isn't new, the specific approach of explicitly modeling the conditional transition distribution and especially the noisy speech diffusion is a valuable contribution. The authors present a methodical advancement, expanding upon current methods with clear methodological alterations.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the increased robustness to domain shifts and improved performance compared to existing unsupervised methods. The unsupervised setting is vital for real-world applications where paired clean and noisy data are limited. The potential to match or surpass supervised baselines in certain mismatched conditions is promising. The removal of the hyperparameter needed for balancing the prior is also a substantial advantage for practical applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Principled approach to posterior sampling, eliminating the need for hyperparameter tuning.
    *   Tractable likelihood computation in DEPSE-TL.
    *   Improved enhancement metrics and robustness to domain shifts, particularly with DEPSE-TL.
    *   Clear presentation and explanation of the algorithms.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with standard datasets and metrics.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper's methods still do not beat SOTA when matched training/test data is used.
    *   Even the presented enhanced methods of this paper are not necessarily better at SI-SIR than those methods currently available.
    *   The introduction of only two methods may seem limited.
    *   Results are shown on only two datasets (though these are commonly used).

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to impact the field of speech enhancement by providing a more robust and reliable unsupervised diffusion-based method. The explicit modeling of the conditional transition distribution could be adopted in other inverse problems as well. The method contributes to the more general field of generative modeling, while simultaneously providing a tool for SE.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a clear advance over previous unsupervised diffusion-based speech enhancement methods. The main weakness is that it isn't necessarily as good as supervised learning, as the supervised algorithm Conv-TasNet is very robust. The improvement in robustness and the elimination of a potentially difficult-to-tune hyperparameter are significant advantages. The overall approach shows strong promise for practical applications.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### PosDiffAE: Position-aware Diffusion Auto-encoder For High-Resolution Brain Tissue Classification Incorporating Artifact Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02405v1)
- **Authors**: Ayantika Das, Moitreya Chaudhuri, Koushik Bhat, Keerthi Ram, Mihail Bota, Mohanasankar Sivaprakasam
- **Abstract**: Denoising diffusion models produce high-fidelity image samples by capturing the image distribution in a progressive manner while initializing with a simple distribution and compounding the distribution complexity. Although these models have unlocked new applicabilities, the sampling mechanism of diffusion does not offer means to extract image-specific semantic representation, which is inherently provided by auto-encoders. The encoding component of auto-encoders enables mapping between a specific image and its latent space, thereby offering explicit means of enforcing structures in the latent space. By integrating an encoder with the diffusion model, we establish an auto-encoding formulation, which learns image-specific representations and offers means to organize the latent space. In this work, First, we devise a mechanism to structure the latent space of a diffusion auto-encoding model, towards recognizing region-specific cellular patterns in brain images. We enforce the representations to regress positional information of the patches from high-resolution images. This creates a conducive latent space for differentiating tissue types of the brain. Second, we devise an unsupervised tear artifact restoration technique based on neighborhood awareness, utilizing latent representations and the constrained generation capability of diffusion models during inference. Third, through representational guidance and leveraging the inference time steerable noising and denoising capability of diffusion, we devise an unsupervised JPEG artifact restoration technique.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "PosDiffAE," a novel position-aware diffusion autoencoder for high-resolution brain tissue classification and artifact restoration. It structures the latent space of a diffusion autoencoder by enforcing representations to regress positional information of image patches, enabling differentiation of brain tissue types. The paper also presents unsupervised techniques for tear artifact and JPEG compression artifact restoration using latent representations, neighborhood awareness, and the steerable denoising capabilities of diffusion models. The model is validated on fetal and adult human brain images, demonstrating multi-tasking capabilities (region classification, position regression, artifact restoration), robustness to artifacts, and generalization ability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in several key aspects:

1.  **Position-Aware Latent Space:**  Structuring the latent space of a diffusion autoencoder using positional information is a significant contribution. While diffusion models have proven powerful for image generation, extracting semantic representations has been a challenge. This structured latent space facilitates downstream tasks like region classification and artifact restoration by leveraging the inherent spatial context of the tissue.
2.  **Unsupervised Artifact Restoration with Context:** The tear and JPEG artifact restoration techniques are novel in their unsupervised approach and the incorporation of contextual information from neighboring patches. This allows for more consistent and realistic restoration, especially compared to patch-based inpainting methods. The adaptive noising strategy for JPEG artifact removal is a clever adaptation of diffusion model capabilities.
3.  **Multi-Tasking Capability:** The integration of region classification, position regression, and artifact restoration within a single framework is noteworthy. It showcases the versatility of the learned representations and the potential for a unified approach to brain tissue image analysis.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work is that it provides a practical and effective means to deal with the inherent challenges present in HR histology image analysis.

1.  **Improved Brain Tissue Analysis:** Accurate brain tissue classification is crucial for neuroscience research, disease diagnosis, and understanding brain development. PosDiffAE offers a robust tool for this purpose, potentially improving the accuracy and efficiency of image analysis pipelines.
2.  **Artifact Mitigation:** Artifacts are a common problem in histological imaging, hindering automated analysis. The presented artifact restoration techniques offer a valuable solution, enabling more reliable data extraction and potentially reducing the need for manual correction.
3.  **Reduced Annotation Burden:** The unsupervised nature of the artifact restoration techniques reduces the dependence on manual annotation. This is particularly beneficial in the context of digital pathology, where large labeled datasets are often scarce.
4.  **Generalization:** The results demonstrate the model's ability to generalize across different datasets (Allen BrainSpan and in-house data) and acquisition settings (fetal and adult brains, coronal and sagittal sections), showcasing its practical utility and applicability to diverse research scenarios.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Technical Contribution:** The integration of diffusion models, autoencoders, and positional information is well-executed and theoretically sound.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation across multiple tasks, datasets, and artifact conditions. The use of appropriate metrics and statistical analysis strengthens the validity of the results.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the methodology and experimental setup.
*   **Replicability:** The provided code availability contributes to the reproducibility of the work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limitations in Highly Dense Regions:**  The paper acknowledges that HIPT models perform better in very dense regions (Ventricular Zone) due to their ability to capture global contextual correlations. This highlights an area for potential future improvement.
*   **Limited Artifact Types:**  The current work focuses on tear, JPEG, and black-dot artifacts.  Expanding to other common artifacts (e.g., water bubbles, uneven staining) would further enhance the model's practical utility.
*   **Inference Time:** While the paper mentions the inference time, a more detailed analysis and discussion of the computational cost of the proposed method, especially for high-resolution images, would be beneficial.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper does not explicitly address the sensitivity of the model's performance to the choice of hyperparameters, which can be a relevant issue for diffusion-based models. A brief analysis of this would strengthen the paper.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of brain tissue image analysis, offering a novel and effective framework for region classification and artifact restoration. The structured latent space and unsupervised artifact restoration techniques are particularly noteworthy. While some limitations exist, the strengths of the work outweigh the weaknesses, making it a valuable addition to the literature.

Score: 8

**Justification for the Score:**

A score of 8 reflects the significant novelty and potential impact of this research. The paper tackles a practical problem in digital pathology and provides a technically sound and well-evaluated solution. The incorporation of positional information into the diffusion autoencoder framework and the unsupervised artifact restoration are strong contributions. The weaknesses, while present, are relatively minor and represent avenues for future research, rather than fundamental flaws. The potential for improved brain tissue analysis and reduced annotation burden warrants a high score, but the limitations described above prevent it from reaching a truly exceptional level (9 or 10). The paper shows how latent spaces learned in an unsupervised fashion can be used to perform downstream tasks effectively.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Improving Consistency in Vehicle Trajectory Prediction Through Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02406v1)
- **Authors**: Caio Azevedo, Lina Achaji, Stefano Sabatini, Nicola Poerio, Grzegorz Bartyzel, Sascha Hornauer, Fabien Moutarde
- **Abstract**: Trajectory prediction is an essential step in the pipeline of an autonomous vehicle. Inaccurate or inconsistent predictions regarding the movement of agents in its surroundings lead to poorly planned maneuvers and potentially dangerous situations for the end-user. Current state-of-the-art deep-learning-based trajectory prediction models can achieve excellent accuracy on public datasets. However, when used in more complex, interactive scenarios, they often fail to capture important interdependencies between agents, leading to inconsistent predictions among agents in the traffic scene. Inspired by the efficacy of incorporating human preference into large language models, this work fine-tunes trajectory prediction models in multi-agent settings using preference optimization. By taking as input automatically calculated preference rankings among predicted futures in the fine-tuning process, our experiments--using state-of-the-art models on three separate datasets--show that we are able to significantly improve scene consistency while minimally sacrificing trajectory prediction accuracy and without adding any excess computational requirements at inference time.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty, significance, and a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a method to improve the consistency of vehicle trajectory prediction models by incorporating preference optimization, drawing inspiration from similar techniques used in large language models (LLMs). The core idea is to fine-tune existing trajectory prediction models (both marginal and joint predictors) by using automatically generated preference rankings based on collision detection and trajectory deviation from ground truth. This ranking is then used to fine-tune the model with an adapted SimPO (Simple Preference Optimization) objective, aiming to reduce collisions and improve overall scene consistency. The experiments on three datasets (Argoverse 2, Interaction, and Waymo Open Motion Dataset) show a reduction in collision rates with minimal impact on trajectory prediction accuracy. The paper explores oversampling techniques to further enhance collision avoidance and investigates the effects of hyperparameter tuning on the proposed approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The idea of applying preference optimization, specifically SimPO, to trajectory prediction is relatively novel.  While preference learning and reward shaping have been explored in robotics and autonomous driving (particularly in reinforcement learning contexts), directly adapting and applying SimPO, a method designed for LLMs, to fine-tune trajectory predictors based on automatically generated preferences is a unique contribution. The adaptation of the SimPO loss function to handle trajectory ranking instead of pairwise comparison adds to the technical novelty.  The use of automated collision-based ranking is clever and removes the need for manual data labeling, which could be cumbersome for trajectory prediction. However, using collision as the preference is a straight-forward but somewhat limited proxy.

**Significance:**

Improving the consistency of trajectory prediction is undeniably significant for autonomous driving safety. Inconsistent predictions can lead to dangerous planning decisions.  The paper addresses this crucial problem by demonstrating that readily available trajectory prediction models can be enhanced using this preference optimization approach. The relatively simple fine-tuning strategy is valuable as it requires minimal additional computational resources at inference time, addressing a key practical concern. While the performance trade-offs (slight accuracy degradation for substantial collision reduction) need careful consideration, the overall direction of improvement is beneficial.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of inconsistent trajectory predictions and motivates its relevance to autonomous driving safety.
*   **Novel approach:** Applying SimPO to trajectory prediction and leveraging automated collision-based preferences is a unique contribution.
*   **Empirical validation:** The experiments on three datasets are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach across different scenarios.
*   **Practical considerations:** The focus on fine-tuning and minimal inference overhead makes the proposed method practically applicable.
*   **Ablation studies:** The hyperparameter tuning experiments and analysis of oversampling provide valuable insights into the behavior of the proposed method.
*   **Writing and clarity:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, making it accessible to a broad audience.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Preference Metric:** The reliance on collision detection as the sole basis for preference ranking is a potential limitation. More sophisticated preference metrics could incorporate factors like comfort, smoothness, adherence to traffic rules, or proximity to other agents even *before* a collision occurs. This could potentially lead to earlier detection and mitigation of potentially unsafe scenarios. Using just collision could be very short-sighted.
*   **Incremental Improvements:** While the collision rate reductions are significant, the degradation in distance-based metrics raises some concerns. Future work should focus on minimizing this trade-off, or perhaps exploring alternative metrics that better reflect the overall quality of trajectory predictions. The degradation could arise from SimPO pushing the output trajectories closer to agents, but still not colliding.
*   **Oversimplification:** The paper assumes that multi-modality arises only from "intentional" agents with multiple goals, whereas reality is more complicated. For example, the trajectory might not be the agent's "best option". Agents might be facing a situation where there are limited maneuverability choices.
*   **Focus on Collision Avoidance:** By focusing on collision detection, the model might fail to learn certain types of non-optimal but acceptable behavior.
*   **Lack of Comparison with More Sophisticated Joint Models:** While comparisons are made with FJMP-Joint (which seems to suffer from mode collapse), comparison with other recent and robust joint prediction models could strengthen the paper.
*  **No guarantees:** Despite mitigating collisions at training, there is no guarantee of the model not encountering collisions in real-world use.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by demonstrating a novel and practical approach to improving the consistency of trajectory prediction models.  The idea of adapting preference optimization from LLMs is insightful, and the empirical results are promising. However, the limitations in preference ranking (solely on collision) and certain compromises with the existing performance hold it back from being truly groundbreaking. Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned. The significance is clear, the novelty adequate, but there is room for substantial improvements in the approach, preference metric sophistication, and more comprehensive comparisons with state-of-the-art models.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AvatarMakeup: Realistic Makeup Transfer for 3D Animatable Head Avatars
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02419v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Zhong, Xiaolin Zhang, Ligang Liu, Yao Zhao, Yunchao Wei
- **Abstract**: Similar to facial beautification in real life, 3D virtual avatars require personalized customization to enhance their visual appeal, yet this area remains insufficiently explored. Although current 3D Gaussian editing methods can be adapted for facial makeup purposes, these methods fail to meet the fundamental requirements for achieving realistic makeup effects: 1) ensuring a consistent appearance during drivable expressions, 2) preserving the identity throughout the makeup process, and 3) enabling precise control over fine details. To address these, we propose a specialized 3D makeup method named AvatarMakeup, leveraging a pretrained diffusion model to transfer makeup patterns from a single reference photo of any individual. We adopt a coarse-to-fine idea to first maintain the consistent appearance and identity, and then to refine the details. In particular, the diffusion model is employed to generate makeup images as supervision. Due to the uncertainties in diffusion process, the generated images are inconsistent across different viewpoints and expressions. Therefore, we propose a Coherent Duplication method to coarsely apply makeup to the target while ensuring consistency across dynamic and multiview effects. Coherent Duplication optimizes a global UV map by recoding the averaged facial attributes among the generated makeup images. By querying the global UV map, it easily synthesizes coherent makeup guidance from arbitrary views and expressions to optimize the target avatar. Given the coarse makeup avatar, we further enhance the makeup by incorporating a Refinement Module into the diffusion model to achieve high makeup quality. Experiments demonstrate that AvatarMakeup achieves state-of-the-art makeup transfer quality and consistency throughout animation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AvatarMakeup: Realistic Makeup Transfer for 3D Animatable Head Avatars."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AvatarMakeup, a new method for transferring makeup styles from a reference image to a 3D animatable head avatar.
