The method addresses the challenges of maintaining consistent appearance during different expressions, preserving identity, and enabling precise control over makeup details. AvatarMakeup uses a coarse-to-fine strategy leveraging a pre-trained diffusion model (Stable-Makeup) to generate makeup guidance. It introduces a Coherent Duplication method to coarsely apply makeup while ensuring consistency across dynamic and multiview effects by optimizing a global UV map.  A Refinement Module is then integrated to enhance the makeup details, improving overall quality.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of AvatarMakeup in achieving state-of-the-art makeup transfer quality and consistency during animation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper exhibits reasonable novelty.  Prior work has explored image-based makeup transfer and 3D avatar editing, but the combination of a coarse-to-fine approach with UV map-based consistency and diffusion-based refinement for *animatable* 3D Gaussian avatars is a key distinguishing factor. The Coherent Duplication method, specifically, addresses a critical problem with directly applying 2D makeup transfer techniques to 3D animatable avatars by ensuring consistent appearances across different views and expressions. The idea of leveraging the UV map to project average facial attributes can be said as innovative in the context of animation.
* **Significance:** The work has potential significance in the field of 3D avatar creation and animation.  Personalized and realistic avatar customization is increasingly important for VR/AR, gaming, and metaverse applications.  AvatarMakeup addresses a practical need for enhancing the visual appeal of 3D avatars while maintaining their identity and animatability. The method's ability to transfer makeup from a single reference photo is also a valuable feature. However, the method relies heavily on Stable-Makeup, and so, if Stable-Makeup is biased by any mean, the whole method becomes flawed.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Coarse-to-fine approach:** The two-stage approach effectively separates the problem into ensuring global consistency and then refining details, leading to improved results.
    * **Coherent Duplication:**  The UV map-based method provides a robust solution for maintaining consistent appearance across different viewpoints and expressions.
    * **Refinement Module:** The integration of a diffusion model allows for fine-grained control and high-quality makeup details.
    * **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper includes quantitative and qualitative evaluations comparing AvatarMakeup to existing methods.
    * The AvatarMakeup works efficiently and can handle high-quality makeup avatars with negligible artifacts.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Stable-Makeup:** The method's performance is heavily dependent on the quality and capabilities of the pre-trained Stable-Makeup model.  Limitations of Stable-Makeup might translate to limitations in AvatarMakeup.
    * **UV map resolution:** The limited resolution of the UV map could potentially restrict the level of detail that can be captured in the base makeup.
    * **Lack of detailed analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the limitations of the method, such as potential failure cases or specific makeup styles that are difficult to transfer accurately.
    * **Implementation details:** Some implementation details could be more explicit, which restricts the method's reproducibility.

* **Impact:** The paper is likely to influence future research on 3D avatar customization and makeup transfer. The proposed method could be adopted as a baseline for comparison or further developed to address its limitations. The coarse-to-fine approach and the Coherent Duplication technique could be useful in other applications requiring consistent appearance across multiple viewpoints or animation frames.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, and balancing the novelty of the method and its potential significance, a score of **7.5** is assigned.

* **The positive aspects** include the reasonable degree of novelty in the combination of techniques for animatable avatars and the effectiveness of the Coherent Duplication method. The method presents a comprehensive framework for realistically customizing 3D avatar appearances via makeup transfer.

* **The negative aspects** are the reliance on a pre-existing diffusion model which may limit its applicability, and a lack of in-depth analysis in its limitations, which reduces the overall potential influence. Despite its limitations, it addresses a genuine problem and proposes a solution demonstrating SOTA performance, which explains the score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Toward a Robust and Generalizable Metamaterial Foundation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02436v1)
- **Authors**: Namjung Kim, Dongseok Lee, Jongbin Yu, Sung Woong Cho, Dosung Lee, Yesol Park, Youngjoon Hong
- **Abstract**: Advances in material functionalities drive innovations across various fields, where metamaterials-defined by structure rather than composition-are leading the way. Despite the rise of artificial intelligence (AI)-driven design strategies, their impact is limited by task-specific retraining, poor out-of-distribution(OOD) generalization, and the need for separate models for forward and inverse design. To address these limitations, we introduce the Metamaterial Foundation Model (MetaFO), a Bayesian transformer-based foundation model inspired by large language models. MetaFO learns the underlying mechanics of metamaterials, enabling probabilistic, zero-shot predictions across diverse, unseen combinations of material properties and structural responses. It also excels in nonlinear inverse design, even under OOD conditions. By treating metamaterials as an operator that maps material properties to structural responses, MetaFO uncovers intricate structure-property relationships and significantly expands the design space. This scalable and generalizable framework marks a paradigm shift in AI-driven metamaterial discovery, paving the way for next-generation innovations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Toward a Robust and Generalizable Metamaterial Foundation Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaFO, a Metamaterial Foundation Operator, leveraging a Bayesian transformer architecture to create a generalizable model for metamaterial design. Inspired by large language models, MetaFO learns the underlying mechanics of metamaterials, enabling probabilistic, zero-shot predictions across diverse material properties and structural responses. It demonstrates capabilities in forward prediction, extrapolation, noise reduction, and inverse design, even under out-of-distribution conditions.  The model treats metamaterials as operators mapping material properties to structural responses, learning intricate relationships and expanding the design space. The authors argue this approach marks a paradigm shift towards AI-driven metamaterial discovery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Generalizability:** A key strength lies in MetaFO's ability to generalize across different metamaterial families and property objectives.  This addresses a significant limitation of existing AI-driven metamaterial design methods, which often require retraining for new tasks. The zero-shot inference capability is particularly valuable.
    *   **Robustness:** The model demonstrates robustness to noisy data and missing information, making it suitable for real-world applications where experimental data may be imperfect or incomplete. The noise reduction capabilities are noteworthy.
    *   **Unified Framework:** The unification of forward and inverse design within a single model architecture is a significant improvement over traditional approaches that require separate models for each task. This increases efficiency and reduces computational costs.
    *   **Bayesian Transformer Architecture:** The integration of Bayesian principles into the transformer architecture via PDFNs adds a layer of predictive robustness, allowing the model to leverage prior knowledge and define prior distributions over unseen material configurations.
    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper directly addresses the limitations of current AI-driven material design, which is task-specific retraining, poor out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization, and the need for separate models for forward and inverse design.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper highlights efficiency gains, the computational cost associated with training and deploying such a large transformer-based model is not explicitly addressed.  The pre-training phase, while done only once, likely demands substantial resources.
    *   **Limited Scope of Material Properties:** The training dataset comprises 10 materials and a finite number of unit cell configurations. This could limit the model's ability to handle materials and structures significantly outside of this defined domain. The materials used in this work are 3D-printable elastomers, and the findings might not translate well to other materials.
    *   **Reliance on Simulated Data:** The training data relies heavily on simulations, which may not perfectly capture the complexities of real-world experiments.  Although the model demonstrates robustness to noise, validating the model on real experimental data would significantly strengthen the claims.
    *   **Limited Detailed Analysis:** While the performance metrics show promising results, a more in-depth analysis of the learned representations and the underlying mechanics captured by MetaFO would provide further insight into its capabilities. It will be interesting to see the relationship among various materials on its potential applications, but it is lacking.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The application of transformer-based architectures and Bayesian principles to create a generalizable foundation model for metamaterial design is relatively novel.
    *   The ability to perform zero-shot inference and handle noisy/incomplete data has significant practical implications for accelerating materials discovery and design.
    *   MetaFO offers a pathway for more efficient and targeted exploration of high-dimensional design spaces in metamaterials.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could inspire further research into foundation models for materials science, potentially leading to the development of more powerful and versatile AI-driven design tools.
    *   MetaFO's approach could be extended to other areas of materials science beyond metamaterials.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of AI-driven metamaterial design by addressing key limitations of existing methods. The generalizability, robustness, and unified framework of MetaFO are valuable contributions. However, the reliance on simulated data, the limited scope of material properties, and lack of insight into relationships between the findings and potential applications suggest that the paper still faces some challenges for real-world applicability. It is a first step in an important direction.

### System-performance and cost modeling of Large Language Model training and inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02456v1)
- **Authors**: Wenzhe Guo, Joyjit Kundu, Uras Tos, Weijiang Kong, Giuliano Sisto, Timon Evenblij, Manu Perumkunnil
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs), based on transformer architectures, have revolutionized numerous domains within artificial intelligence, science, and engineering due to their exceptional scalability and adaptability. However, the exponential growth in LLM size and complexity has outpaced advancements in compute capacity, memory bandwidth, network performance, and cost efficiency, posing significant challenges to their scalability on distributed systems. To address these limitations, alternative model architectures, optimization strategies, communication-aware network topologies, and novel system design approaches have been proposed in literature. This paper introduces a performance-cost modeling methodology for LLM training and inference that integrates state-of-the-art compute techniques with memory optimizations, and latest communication techniques. Building on an analytical performance model, our approach incorporates recent innovations such as the flash attention technique and mixture of experts models to address the memory bandwidth and compute bottlenecks. It also considers the impact of different network topologies and topology-specific communication algorithms with 5D parallellism. The framework also integrates a chiplet cost model. The proposed modeling methodology provides valuable insights to guide future compute system design and facilitates hardware-software co-development, in particular due to its ability to analyze performance-cost trade-offs for various system architectural configurations.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary of the paper, along with a critical evaluation and score:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a performance and cost modeling methodology for large language model (LLM) training and inference on distributed systems. It expands upon existing frameworks by incorporating state-of-the-art techniques like FlashAttention and Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models, along with topology-specific communication algorithms, and integrates a chiplet-based cost model. The authors validate their model against published data and present case studies illustrating how the framework can be used to explore performance-cost trade-offs for various system architectural configurations, including choices in hardware, interconnect and software. The framework aims to guide compute system design and facilitate hardware-software co-development for LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Modeling:** The paper successfully integrates multiple key aspects of LLM performance, going beyond prior works that often focus on only one or two areas. By combining compute kernel modeling (FlashAttention, MoE), network communication modeling (various topologies and collective algorithms), and cost modeling, the framework enables a more holistic view of system design.
    *   **Integration of Chiplet Cost Model:** The inclusion of a chiplet-based cost model is a significant addition. While performance modeling is important, understanding the economic implications is crucial for practical system design. This allows for true cost-performance optimization.
    *   **Validation:** The validation against published data provides confidence in the accuracy and relevance of the model.  Validation with multiple datasets, including those for FlashAttention, MoE, and network performance, lends credibility to the framework.
    *   **Case Studies:** The case studies effectively demonstrate how the framework can be used to explore design choices and identify trade-offs. The A100 GPU based case study is particularly illustrative.  The insight on the relationship between FlashAttention, memory bandwidth, and compute is valuable. The inclusion of expert parallelism expands the parallelism space to 5D, a potential design space that hasn't been sufficiently explored.
    * **Well-written and clear**: The paper is well-written and presented, making it easy to understand the methodology and results. The figures are helpful in visualizing the different components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Cost Model:** While the inclusion of a chiplet cost model is commendable, it primarily focuses on chip manufacturing and assembly. A more comprehensive total cost of ownership (TCO) model would be valuable, incorporating factors like power consumption, cooling, and data center infrastructure. The cost model also doesn't include the complexities of software development and support costs.
    *   **Abstraction Level:** The analytical nature of the performance model means that it is based on abstractions and simplifications. While necessary for scalability, these abstractions might miss some subtle micro-architectural details or workload-specific behaviors. The accuracy depends on the fidelity of the underlying models and parameters.
    *   **Limited novelty in the Chiplet Cost Model**. The reliance on an existing chiplet cost model [29] diminishes the novelty of the integrated cost-performance model. However, the integration of an existing model into a broader framework is an important engineering step.
    *   **Limited evaluation against real-world systems:** Validation primarily relies on published data, which can be limited. More extensive validation against real-world systems would enhance the credibility of the framework.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The framework provides a valuable tool for system architects and hardware designers working on LLM acceleration. It can help them make informed decisions about hardware configurations, network topologies, and software optimizations, leading to more efficient and cost-effective LLM deployments.
    *   The framework enables hardware-software co-design by allowing designers to explore the interactions between different hardware and software components. This can lead to new innovations in both hardware and software.
    *   The integrated framework fills a gap in the literature, providing a more holistic view of system design for LLMs. By combining performance modeling, communication modeling, and cost modeling, it enables more informed decision-making.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a significant engineering contribution by integrating several important aspects of LLM system design into a single framework. While the cost model could be more comprehensive, and the performance model relies on abstractions, the overall approach is valuable and addresses a real-world need in the LLM acceleration space. It provides a tool that will enable researchers and practitioners to better evaluate and optimize their systems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper is a solid contribution that combines several existing methods and integrates them into a useful tool. The framework is comprehensive and well-validated. However, the limited scope of the cost model and the reliance on existing chiplet cost model slightly reduce the novelty. The paper is practical and addresses an immediate need in the field, which is reflected in the score.

### MedFormer: Hierarchical Medical Vision Transformer with Content-Aware Dual Sparse Selection Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02488v1)
- **Authors**: Zunhui Xia, Hongxing Li, Libin Lan
- **Abstract**: Medical image recognition serves as a key way to aid in clinical diagnosis, enabling more accurate and timely identification of diseases and abnormalities. Vision transformer-based approaches have proven effective in handling various medical recognition tasks. However, these methods encounter two primary challenges. First, they are often task-specific and architecture-tailored, limiting their general applicability. Second, they usually either adopt full attention to model long-range dependencies, resulting in high computational costs, or rely on handcrafted sparse attention, potentially leading to suboptimal performance. To tackle these issues, we present MedFormer, an efficient medical vision transformer with two key ideas. First, it employs a pyramid scaling structure as a versatile backbone for various medical image recognition tasks, including image classification and dense prediction tasks such as semantic segmentation and lesion detection. This structure facilitates hierarchical feature representation while reducing the computation load of feature maps, highly beneficial for boosting performance. Second, it introduces a novel Dual Sparse Selection Attention (DSSA) with content awareness to improve computational efficiency and robustness against noise while maintaining high performance. As the core building technique of MedFormer, DSSA is explicitly designed to attend to the most relevant content. In addition, a detailed theoretical analysis has been conducted, demonstrating that MedFormer has superior generality and efficiency in comparison to existing medical vision transformers. Extensive experiments on a variety of imaging modality datasets consistently show that MedFormer is highly effective in enhancing performance across all three above-mentioned medical image recognition tasks. The code is available at https://github.com/XiaZunhui/MedFormer.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "MedFormer: Hierarchical Medical Vision Transformer with Content-Aware Dual Sparse Selection Attention":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MedFormer, a new vision transformer architecture designed for medical image recognition tasks. It addresses limitations of existing methods by proposing a task-agnostic, modality-invariant, and quality-adaptive model. MedFormer features a hierarchical pyramid structure that reduces computational costs and enables multi-scale feature extraction.  The core innovation is the Dual Sparse Selection Attention (DSSA) mechanism, which dynamically and content-awarely selects relevant features at both region and pixel levels, aiming to improve computational efficiency and robustness against noise. The authors present extensive experimental results across various medical imaging modalities (MRI, CT, etc.) and tasks (classification, segmentation, detection), demonstrating improved performance compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Task Agnosticism:** A key strength is the design of MedFormer as a general-purpose backbone for diverse medical image recognition tasks, unlike many existing methods that are tailored to specific applications. This is a significant contribution, promoting broader applicability.
    *   **Dual Sparse Selection Attention (DSSA):** The DSSA mechanism is a well-motivated approach for reducing the computational burden of transformers while maintaining performance. The content-aware selection process is designed to mitigate the impact of noise, which is a prevalent issue in medical images.  The theoretical analysis of DSSA's computational complexity is valuable.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive experimental evaluation on a wide range of datasets and tasks. This rigorous evaluation demonstrates the effectiveness of MedFormer and allows for fair comparisons with existing methods. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Performance Improvements:**  The experimental results consistently show that MedFormer achieves superior performance or competitive performance with a significant computational efficiency compared to the other methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While DSSA is a useful addition, the overall architecture relies on well-established techniques like pyramid structures and sparse attention. The novelty, therefore, lies primarily in the specific combination and adaptation of these techniques for medical imaging, rather than a groundbreaking new concept. Many sparse attention mechanisms already existed, so claiming significant "novelty" can be contentious.
    *   **Complexity:** The algorithm itself is complex, and a simple backbone that is just as or more effective would be more useful in real-world clinical practice.
    *   **Limited Availability of Code:** The paper mentions that the code is available on Github, however it cannot be assessed without it.

*   **Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its attempt to provide a general and efficient vision transformer architecture for medical image recognition. The MedFormer model and its DSSA mechanism addresses real-world challenges associated with the data and computation. The extensive evaluation could influence future research to create more flexible backbone architectures for computer-aided diagnosis. However, the practical impact and adoption rate will depend on how much value the community places on achieving generalized performance rather than task-specific improvements.

* **Novelty Score Rationale:**

The architecture is a combination of known techniques adapted for a specific domain. The dual sparse selection attention is a well-engineered but incremental advance. The extensive experimentation does however demonstrate a practical value. The theoretical analysis provides some support for the improved efficiency.
Therefore, the work is deemed as **good but not exceptional**.

### Continual Gradient Low-Rank Projection Fine-Tuning for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02503v1)
- **Authors**: Chenxu Wang, Yilin Lyu, Zicheng Sun, Liping Jing
- **Abstract**: Continual fine-tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs) is hampered by the trade-off between efficiency and expressiveness. Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) offers efficiency but constrains the model's ability to learn new tasks and transfer knowledge due to its low-rank nature and reliance on explicit parameter constraints. We propose GORP (Gradient LOw Rank Projection) for Continual Learning, a novel training strategy that overcomes these limitations by synergistically combining full and low-rank parameters and jointly updating within a unified low-rank gradient subspace. GORP expands the optimization space while preserving efficiency and mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Extensive experiments on continual learning benchmarks demonstrate GORP's superior performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches. Code is available at https://github.com/Wcxwcxw/GORP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GORP (Gradient Low-Rank Projection), a novel training strategy for continual fine-tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs). GORP aims to address the trade-off between efficiency and expressiveness in continual learning. It combines full and low-rank parameter updates within a low-rank gradient subspace, expanding the optimization space while preserving efficiency and mitigating catastrophic forgetting. The core idea is based on the observation that gradients tend to adopt a low-rank structure during training. Experimental results on continual learning benchmarks demonstrate GORP's superior performance compared to existing state-of-the-art approaches. The paper argues that GORP effectively balances stability and plasticity in continual learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a crucial challenge: balancing efficiency and expressiveness in continual learning for LLMs, especially the catastrophic forgetting problem.
    *   The proposed method, GORP, offers a novel way to integrate full and low-rank parameter updates. This combines the strengths of both approaches, expanding the search space while maintaining efficiency.
    *   The idea of leveraging first-order moment of gradients to capture the dynamic properties of the gradient space seems promising. It reduces computational complexity compared to directly manipulating the hidden feature space.
    *   The experimental results on multiple continual learning benchmarks demonstrate the superior performance of GORP compared to existing methods, validating its effectiveness. The visualizations and ablation study also provide useful insights.
    *   The paper considers the computational overhead and proposes strategies for mitigation.
    * Addresses the limitation of prior methods by implicitly capturing the dynamic properties of gradient space by utilizing the first-order moment of gradients

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The technical novelty, while present, might not be revolutionary. Gradient projection techniques are not entirely new. The specific combination of full/low-rank updates and the gradient space construction are the key contributions, and the degree of novelty should be accurately presented.
    *   The paper could benefit from a deeper theoretical analysis of why GORP works. While the empirical results are convincing, a theoretical justification for the low-rank gradient structure and its impact on continual learning would strengthen the contribution.
    *   The experiments could be more comprehensive, including a broader range of LLMs and continual learning scenarios. Evaluation on real-world tasks would further increase the significance of the results.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of the limitations of GORP. When and why might GORP fail? What are its limitations compared to full fine-tuning?

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper offers a practical and effective solution to a significant problem in continual learning for LLMs.
    *   The idea of combining full and low-rank parameter updates within a low-rank gradient subspace could inspire future research in this area.
    *   The experimental results provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of different continual learning strategies.
    *   The method maintains fine-tuning efficiency in continual learning and balances stability and plasticity more efficiently.

*   **Novelty:** While gradient projection and low-rank adaptation are existing techniques, the specific *combination* of jointly updating full and low-rank parameters within a unified low-rank *gradient subspace* is a novel contribution.  The use of first-order gradient moments for implicit gradient space capture, avoiding explicit constraints, is another noteworthy innovation, departing from parameter regularization strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses a crucial problem with a well-motivated approach and demonstrates its effectiveness through extensive experiments. However, the technical novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, and the theoretical analysis could be deeper. It's a solid contribution but doesn't fundamentally change the field.

### Meta-Fair: AI-Assisted Fairness Testing of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02533v1)
- **Authors**: Miguel Romero-Arjona, José A. Parejo, Juan C. Alonso, Ana B. Sánchez, Aitor Arrieta, Sergio Segura
- **Abstract**: Fairness--the absence of unjustified bias--is a core principle in the development of Artificial Intelligence (AI) systems, yet it remains difficult to assess and enforce. Current approaches to fairness testing in large language models (LLMs) often rely on manual evaluation, fixed templates, deterministic heuristics, and curated datasets, making them resource-intensive and difficult to scale. This work aims to lay the groundwork for a novel, automated method for testing fairness in LLMs, reducing the dependence on domain-specific resources and broadening the applicability of current approaches. Our approach, Meta-Fair, is based on two key ideas. First, we adopt metamorphic testing to uncover bias by examining how model outputs vary in response to controlled modifications of input prompts, defined by metamorphic relations (MRs). Second, we propose exploiting the potential of LLMs for both test case generation and output evaluation, leveraging their capability to generate diverse inputs and classify outputs effectively. The proposal is complemented by three open-source tools supporting LLM-driven generation, execution, and evaluation of test cases. We report the findings of several experiments involving 12 pre-trained LLMs, 14 MRs, 5 bias dimensions, and 7.9K automatically generated test cases. The results show that Meta-Fair is effective in uncovering bias in LLMs, achieving an average precision of 92% and revealing biased behaviour in 29% of executions. Additionally, LLMs prove to be reliable and consistent evaluators, with the best-performing models achieving F1-scores of up to 0.79. Although non-determinism affects consistency, these effects can be mitigated through careful MR design. While challenges remain to ensure broader applicability, the results indicate a promising path towards an unprecedented level of automation in LLM testing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Meta-Fair: AI-Assisted Fairness Testing of Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Meta-Fair, a novel automated approach for fairness testing in Large Language Models (LLMs). Meta-Fair leverages two key ideas: (1) Metamorphic Testing (MT) to uncover bias by examining how model outputs vary in response to controlled modifications of input prompts using metamorphic relations (MRs); and (2) the potential of LLMs for both test case generation and output evaluation. The authors implemented Meta-Fair with three open-source tools (MUSE, GENIE, GUARD-ME) supporting LLM-driven test case generation, execution, and evaluation.  They evaluated Meta-Fair with 12 pre-trained LLMs, 14 MRs, 5 bias dimensions and 7.9K automatically generated test cases. The results show that Meta-Fair can effectively uncover bias, achieving an average precision of 92% and revealing biased behavior in 29% of executions.  LLMs can be reliable evaluators, with the best-performing models reaching F1-scores of up to 0.79. The study indicates that carefully designed MRs can mitigate non-determinism's effects on consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper introduces a significant level of automation to fairness testing of LLMs by integrating MT with LLMs for test case generation and evaluation. While metamorphic testing has been used previously for fairness testing in NLP and LLMs, using LLMs *themselves* to generate test cases and as oracles/judges is relatively novel. The introduction of 13 (or 14, depending on how the baseline is counted) MRs tailored for fairness in LLMs also adds to the paper's novelty.  However, some MRs may be seen as variants of existing MT strategies, but adapted for the specific context of LLM fairness. The open-source tooling supporting the approach provides a tangible resource for the community.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a critical challenge in the responsible development of AI: fairness.  Current fairness testing approaches are often resource-intensive and difficult to scale. Meta-Fair's ability to automate test case generation and evaluation provides a way to more efficiently and thoroughly test LLMs for bias. The empirical evaluation, involving a substantial number of LLMs and test cases, supports the claim that the approach is effective. The public availability of the tools and data enhances the potential for further research and adoption of the proposed approach. A weakness, however, is the relatively limited scope of the evaluation, focused on a small set of bias dimensions. While the selected dimensions are important, the performance of Meta-Fair on other types of bias may vary.

**Strengths:**

*   **Automation:**  Reduces manual effort in fairness testing.
*   **Scalability:**  LLM-driven generation and evaluation make the approach more scalable than traditional methods.
*   **Effectiveness:**  The empirical results demonstrate the ability to uncover bias.
*   **Open Source Tools:** Provides practical resources for the community.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The use of many models and multiple runs increases the confidence in the results.
* Clear lessons learned and discussion points highlighting important issues and future directions

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Bias Dimensions:**  The evaluation focuses on only five bias dimensions. This could limit the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Dependence on LLM Quality:**  The accuracy of test case generation and evaluation depends on the capabilities and potential biases of the LLMs used for these tasks.
*   **Non-Determinism:** Although the study addresses non-determinism, this remains a challenge in the reliability of LLM-based testing.
* The metric to judge LLM performance is F1-score which is limited because it looks at equal cost between precision and recall, which may not be true for all applications. Similarly, other metrics like the ROC AUC might be more useful depending on the application.
* No explicit mention of explainability. Is the approach explainable or can you find out why a particular bias occurred. This can help with fixing the bias in the models.
* Limited negative results discussion where the approach failed, and failure modes are also not clearly discussed.

**Potential Influence:**

Meta-Fair has the potential to significantly influence the field of AI fairness testing, by promoting a scalable, automated approach to bias detection in LLMs. The open-source tooling can facilitate the adoption of MT in this domain. The research may also inspire further work on improving LLM-based test generation and evaluation, and on developing more robust methods for mitigating the impact of non-determinism.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practically useful approach to a challenging problem. The weaknesses, such as the limited bias dimensions and the dependence on LLM quality, are acknowledged and represent areas for future research. However, the strengths in automation, scalability, and empirical evaluation justify a positive assessment.

### Are You Listening to Me? Fine-Tuning Chatbots for Empathetic Dialogue
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02537v1)
- **Authors**: Paulo Ricardo Knob, Leonardo Scholler, Juliano Rigatti, Soraia Raupp Musse
- **Abstract**: Conversational agents have made significant progress since ELIZA, expanding their role across various domains, including healthcare, education, and customer service. As these agents become increasingly integrated into daily human interactions, the need for emotional intelligence, particularly empathetic listening, becomes increasingly essential. In this study, we explore how Large Language Models (LLMs) respond when tasked with generating emotionally rich interactions. Starting from a small dataset manually crafted by an expert to reflect empathic behavior, we extended the conversations using two LLMs: ChatGPT and Gemini. We analyzed the emotional progression of the dialogues using both sentiment analysis (via VADER) and expert assessments. While the generated conversations often mirrored the intended emotional structure, human evaluation revealed important differences in the perceived empathy and coherence of the responses. These findings suggest that emotion modeling in dialogues requires not only structural alignment in the expressed emotions but also qualitative depth, highlighting the importance of combining automated and humancentered methods in the development of emotionally competent agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Are You Listening to Me? Fine-Tuning Chatbots for Empathetic Dialogue" explores the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically ChatGPT and Gemini, to generate empathetic responses in dialogues.  The researchers created a dataset of expert-authored empathetic conversations, extended these conversations using the LLMs, and also generated control conversations without explicit empathetic fine-tuning.  They analyzed the emotional progression of these dialogues using sentiment analysis (VADER) and expert human evaluation. The results indicated that while LLMs can structurally mimic empathetic dialogue (increasing emotional energy), they often lack genuine empathetic understanding and qualitative depth, as revealed by human assessment.  The study emphasizes the importance of combining automated and human-centered methods in developing emotionally competent agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The idea of assessing LLMs for empathetic dialogue is not entirely new. Prior work (cited in the paper) has explored similar themes. However, this paper distinguishes itself by:
    *   Comparing two prominent LLMs (ChatGPT and Gemini) in the same experimental setup.
    *   Using both automated sentiment analysis and *human* expert evaluation.
    *   Creating several datasets for comparision, including the expert-authored ones.
    *   Focusing specifically on *empathetic listening*, which is a nuanced aspect of emotional intelligence.
    *   Conducting a preliminary study that serves as a baseline for future research.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its practical implications for developing more emotionally intelligent conversational agents. The findings highlight a crucial gap: LLMs can generate positive sentiment but struggle with genuine understanding and emotional engagement. This is significant for applications where empathy is vital, such as mental health support, customer service, and education. The call for combining automated analysis with human evaluation is particularly relevant.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-defined methodology, outlining dataset creation, extension, sentiment analysis using VADER, and expert questionnaire design.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:**  Comparing ChatGPT and Gemini on the same task provides valuable insights into their respective strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Human Evaluation:**  The inclusion of human expert evaluation is a significant strength. It addresses the limitations of automated sentiment analysis in capturing the nuances of empathy.
    *   **Relevance:** The topic is highly relevant in the context of the increasing integration of conversational agents into daily life.
    *   **Clear Findings and Discussion:** The paper clearly presents the results and discusses their implications, emphasizing the gap between structural alignment and genuine empathy.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** Although the dataset's design is solid, a larger set of manually tailored conversations could strengthens the results.
    *   **Single Expert Evaluation:**  The reliance on a single expert limits the generalizability of the human evaluation.  Multiple expert opinions would provide a more robust assessment.
    *   **VADER Limitations:** While VADER is a suitable tool for initial sentiment assessment, more sophisticated methods of emotion detection that factor in contextual information and subtle emotional cues might enhance analysis.
    *   **Lack of Statistical Significance Testing:** The paper doesn't provide statistical tests to support the claim that the differences between the LLMs are significant. Statistical testing will be more important with larger datasets and human evaluations in the future.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Prompt Engineering:** The paper mentions prompt engineering but does not detail the specific prompts used. A more in-depth discussion of prompt design and its impact on empathetic responses would be beneficial.

* **Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a reasonable contribution to the field of emotionally intelligent conversational agents. Its key strengths are the comparative analysis of two popular LLMs, the inclusion of human evaluation, and the emphasis on the limitations of relying solely on automated sentiment analysis. The weaknesses mainly concern the size of the dataset and the number of human evaluators, which impacts the reliability of the results and the study's generalizability.

Overall, the paper's solid methodology and relevant findings support its contribution to the field, despite its limitations in dataset size and statistical analysis. Therefore, the paper is a decent first step in exploring this area.

### Clarifying Before Reasoning: A Coq Prover with Structural Context
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02541v1)
- **Authors**: Yanzhen Lu, Hanbin Yang, Xiaodie Wang, Ge Zhang, Biao Li, Chenxu Fu, Chao Li, Yang Yuan, Andrew Chi-Chih Yao
- **Abstract**: In this work, we investigate whether improving task clarity can enhance reasoning ability of large language models, focusing on theorem proving in Coq. We introduce a concept-level metric to evaluate task clarity and show that adding structured semantic context to the standard input used by modern LLMs, leads to a 1.85$\times$ improvement in clarity score (44.5\%~$\rightarrow$~82.3\%). Using the general-purpose model \texttt{DeepSeek-V3}, our approach leads to a 2.1$\times$ improvement in proof success (21.8\%~$\rightarrow$~45.8\%) and outperforms the previous state-of-the-art \texttt{Graph2Tac} (33.2\%). We evaluate this on 1,386 theorems randomly sampled from 15 standard Coq packages, following the same evaluation protocol as \texttt{Graph2Tac}. Furthermore, fine-tuning smaller models on our structured data can achieve even higher performance (48.6\%). Our method uses selective concept unfolding to enrich task descriptions, and employs a Planner--Executor architecture. These findings highlight the value of structured task representations in bridging the gap between understanding and reasoning.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel approach to improve the reasoning ability of large language models (LLMs) in the context of theorem proving in Coq by enhancing task clarity through structural context. The core idea is that inadequate task understanding can hinder an LLM's reasoning capabilities, even if the model has sufficient raw reasoning power. To address this, the authors propose a method for enriching the task description by selectively unfolding concepts and providing structured semantic context derived from Coq's internal representation. They introduce a concept-level metric, the "clarity score," to evaluate the model's understanding of a given task. They also leverage a Planner-Executor architecture to separate high-level strategic reasoning from low-level tactic generation. Experiments demonstrate that adding structured semantic context improves clarity scores and significantly boosts proof success rates, outperforming previous state-of-the-art methods. They show the efficiency of their approach by fine-tuning smaller models which achieve comparable results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by shifting the focus from purely scaling models or refining datasets to improving task clarity as a means of enhancing LLM reasoning. The clarity score provides a measurable metric for evaluating task understanding, which is a valuable tool. The structured data extraction from Coq's internal representation is a key component, enabling the LLM to access richer semantic information beyond the raw code.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The paper presents a fresh perspective on improving LLM reasoning, distinct from the conventional emphasis on scaling and reinforcement learning.
*   **Clarity Score Metric:** The clarity score provides a quantitative measure of task understanding, which is a useful diagnostic tool and allows for comparisons between different approaches.
*   **Structured Data Extraction:** The data processing pipeline effectively extracts type-theoretic information from Coq, enriching the task description with relevant semantic context.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate significant improvements in both clarity scores and proof success rates. The fact that they outperform existing state-of-the-art is convincing. The efficiency of smaller fine-tuned models is also promising.
*   **Generalizability:** While focusing on Coq, the underlying principle of improving task clarity through structured information could potentially be applicable to other domains requiring precise reasoning.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experiment design clearly addressed and validated their research questions.
*   **Ablation studies** which revealed the incremental benefits of information components

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Heuristic Strategy for Concept Unfolding:** The selective concept unfolding strategy, while effective, relies on a heuristic. It could benefit from a more principled or adaptive approach for determining which concepts to expand and to what depth. The connection between this work and the underlying theory of the "Yoneda Lemma" isn't fully justified as more than just inspiration.
*   **Complexity of Coq Implementation:** The implementation requires modifying the Coq compiler, which could be a barrier to adoption. Providing more generalizable data gathering techniques may increase practical value.
*   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the dataset covers diverse mathematical domains, it is limited to theorems sampled from standard Coq packages. Testing on more challenging or real-world Coq projects would further validate the approach.
*   **Dependency on LLMs:** While improving task clarity helps, the system still relies on the inherent capabilities of LLMs. A deeper theoretical analysis of the interplay between task clarity and LLM reasoning power would strengthen the work.
*   **Computational Expense:** While this improved accuracy, it is unclear what the increase in processing time to run the models and construct structured contexts. Further work comparing computational performance of other models would increase adoption.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by highlighting the importance of task clarity in LLM reasoning and providing a concrete methodology for enhancing it. The proposed approach could pave the way for more effective and reliable AI systems in domains demanding precise reasoning. The focus on model-agnostic improvements is valuable for broad adoption.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach with compelling experimental results, demonstrating a clear improvement over existing methods. While there are limitations related to the heuristic concept unfolding strategy and the complexity of the Coq implementation, the significance of the findings and the potential impact on the field justify a high score. The work takes a fresh approach and has strong potential to influence future research.

### Transformers Don't Need LayerNorm at Inference Time: Scaling LayerNorm Removal to GPT-2 XL and the Implications for Mechanistic Interpretability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02559v1)
- **Authors**: Luca Baroni, Galvin Khara, Joachim Schaeffer, Marat Subkhankulov, Stefan Heimersheim
- **Abstract**: Layer-wise normalization (LN) is an essential component of virtually all transformer-based large language models. While its effects on training stability are well documented, its role at inference time is poorly understood. Additionally, LN layers hinder mechanistic interpretability by introducing additional nonlinearities and increasing the interconnectedness of individual model components. Here, we show that all LN layers can be removed from every GPT-2 model with only a small increase in validation loss (e.g. +0.03 cross-entropy loss for GPT-2 XL). Thus, LN cannot play a substantial role in language modeling. We find that the amount of fine-tuning data needed for LN removal grows sublinearly with model parameters, suggesting scaling to larger models is feasible. We release a suite of LN-free GPT-2 models on Hugging Face. Furthermore, we test interpretability techniques on LN-free models. Direct logit attribution now gives the exact direct effect of individual components, while the accuracy of attribution patching does not significantly improve. We also confirm that GPT-2's "confidence neurons" are inactive in the LN-free models. Our work clarifies the role of LN layers in language modeling, showing that GPT-2-class models can function without LN layers. We hope that our LN-free analogs of the GPT-2 family of models will enable more precise interpretability research and improve our understanding of language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Transformers Don't Need LayerNorm at Inference Time: Scaling LayerNorm Removal to GPT-2 XL and the Implications for Mechanistic Interpretability":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the role of Layer Normalization (LN) in transformer-based language models, specifically at inference time. The authors demonstrate that LN layers can be removed from GPT-2 models (including the XL variant) with only a small increase in validation loss.  They achieve this through a fine-tuning procedure where LN layers are gradually replaced with linear transformations. The resulting "LN-free" models are then analyzed using interpretability techniques. The authors find that direct logit attribution becomes more accurate in LN-free models, while attribution patching does not improve significantly. They also observe that confidence neurons, previously identified as important, become inactive in the LN-free models. The paper suggests that LN's role at inference might be less critical than previously assumed and that LN-free models can facilitate mechanistic interpretability research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of removing LayerNorm at inference is relatively novel, although previous work has explored training models without LN or approximating LN with linear transformations (cited in the paper). The contribution lies in systematically demonstrating this on a relatively large model (GPT-2 XL), providing a well-defined fine-tuning procedure, and then attempting to measure how the removal of LN improves interpretability. The exploration of LN's effect on confidence neurons adds another dimension to the analysis.

**Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons:

*   **Simplifying Models:** Removing LN simplifies the model architecture, which can be beneficial for deployment (slightly) and for theoretical analysis.

*   **Improving Interpretability:** The biggest claimed significance is the facilitation of mechanistic interpretability. The claim that direct logit attribution becomes *exact* in LN-free models is a strong one, and the authors provide evidence to support it. The counterintuitive finding that attribution patching doesn't improve is also valuable, prompting further investigation into the limitations of that method.

*   **Understanding LN's Role:** The study sheds light on the actual role of LN during inference. Demonstrating it is not as essential as believed encourages rethinking model design and may lead to the development of models with equivalent or superior performance but with a simpler architecture or even faster inference. The finding that confidence neurons are neutered is an interesting side observation relating to LN's role.

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Experiments:** The authors perform thorough experiments across multiple GPT-2 model sizes.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The fine-tuning procedure for removing LN is well-described.
*   **Publicly Available Models:** The release of LN-free GPT-2 models is valuable for other researchers.
*   **Addresses Interpretability Concerns:** Directly tackles a challenge facing mechanistic interpretability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Performance Impact:** While the performance degradation is small, it exists, and the paper doesn't fully explore methods to mitigate this. Further exploration of how best to fine-tune or even train the models without LN from scratch might improve the models.  This somewhat limits the practical impact; the simplified architecture comes at a cost.
*   **Attribution Patching:** The negative result for attribution patching is interesting, but the paper doesn't deeply investigate *why* it fails to improve, beyond the suggestion that other nonlinearities are involved. A more comprehensive analysis would strengthen this finding.
*   **GPT-2 Specificity:** The experiments are limited to GPT-2 models. It's important to consider how well the findings generalize to other transformer architectures, especially those with different LN implementations (e.g., post-LN architectures) or larger models.
*   **Minor Improvement in Interpretability:** The exactness of DLA is somewhat incremental as it serves as a sanity check. The contribution primarily simplifies the calculations rather than providing more insight to the model.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating the feasibility of removing LayerNorm at inference time and exploring the implications for interpretability. The authors provide a solid methodology, systematic experiments, and release useful resources for further research. However, the limited performance impact, the lack of in-depth analysis regarding the failure of attribution patching, and the GPT-2 specificity restrain the paper's overall significance. While improving the exactness of DLA is impactful, the improvements in interpretability provided are not large and do not provide additional insight. The impact of the paper primarily serves as a new baseline for experiments and provides a slight modification to existing techniques.

### LLMREI: Automating Requirements Elicitation Interviews with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02564v1)
- **Authors**: Alexander Korn, Samuel Gorsch, Andreas Vogelsang
- **Abstract**: Requirements elicitation interviews are crucial for gathering system requirements but heavily depend on skilled analysts, making them resource-intensive, susceptible to human biases, and prone to miscommunication. Recent advancements in Large Language Models present new opportunities for automating parts of this process. This study introduces LLMREI, a chat bot designed to conduct requirements elicitation interviews with minimal human intervention, aiming to reduce common interviewer errors and improve the scalability of requirements elicitation. We explored two main approaches, zero-shot prompting and least-to-most prompting, to optimize LLMREI for requirements elicitation and evaluated its performance in 33 simulated stakeholder interviews. A third approach, fine-tuning, was initially considered but abandoned due to poor performance in preliminary trials. Our study assesses the chat bot's effectiveness in three key areas: minimizing common interview errors, extracting relevant requirements, and adapting its questioning based on interview context and user responses. Our findings indicate that LLMREI makes a similar number of errors compared to human interviewers, is capable of extracting a large portion of requirements, and demonstrates a notable ability to generate highly context-dependent questions. We envision the greatest benefit of LLMREI in automating interviews with a large number of stakeholders.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMREI, a chatbot designed to automate requirements elicitation interviews. The authors explore two prompting strategies (zero-shot and least-to-most) using GPT-4o, and initially attempted fine-tuning GPT-3.5, later abandoned due to poor performance. They evaluate LLMREI's ability to minimize interviewer errors, elicit relevant requirements, and adapt its questioning to the context of the conversation, simulating interviews with stakeholders for a hair salon and a ski resort. The results indicate that LLMREI performs comparably to human interviewers in error frequency, can elicit a significant portion of requirements, and demonstrates contextual adaptation in questioning. The authors envision using LLMREI for initial interviews with large numbers of stakeholders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The novelty of the paper lies in its empirical exploration of LLMs for automating requirements elicitation interviews. While LLMs have been applied to various RE tasks, the conversational aspect of elicitation, particularly in a fully automated fashion, is less explored. Evaluating and comparing different prompting strategies specifically for interviews is a contribution. However, other research projects have explored the usage of conversational agents to elicit requirements, which limits the perceived novelty of the approach.

*Significance:*

The significance of this work stems from its potential to improve the scalability and reduce the resource intensity of requirements elicitation. Automating the initial interview phase could allow gathering input from a broader range of stakeholders, potentially improving the completeness of requirements. The identification of effective prompting strategies is also valuable. However, the study has limitations.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem statement:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges in traditional requirements elicitation and proposes a relevant solution.
*   **Rigorous evaluation:** The use of simulated stakeholder interviews and a comparison with trained human interviewers provides a reasonably robust evaluation framework.
*   **Detailed analysis:** The analysis of interviewer errors, requirement elicitation effectiveness, and contextual adaptation provides valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of LLMREI.
*   **Replication package:** Providing the code and data enhances reproducibility and allows for further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited fine-tuning evaluation:** The authors abandoned fine-tuning relatively early. While the reasoning for this abandonment is justified, it is a limitation to explore this aspect more in depth.
*   **Small sample size:** The sample size of 33 interviews limits the statistical significance of the results. A larger study would be needed to confirm the findings.
*   **Simulated interviews:** The use of simulated interviews may not fully capture the complexities of real-world stakeholder interactions, particularly concerning power dynamics, or sensitive topics. The stakeholders, being mostly students and young professionals, may not be representative of a more experienced real-world user base.
*   **Narrow scope:** The study focuses on two specific scenarios. Generalizability to other domains and types of software systems needs to be established.
*  **Hallucination Risk:** The model exhibits a potential risk to generate hallucinations which needs to be fully addressed.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper can influence future research by highlighting the potential of LLMs in automating parts of RE processes and identifying promising prompting strategies. It also points out the limitations and challenges that need to be addressed to make LLM-based approaches more practical. It encourages exploration into self-hosted models, and a greater focus on more diverse stakeholders.

**Justification of the Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable empirical study and provides useful insights into the application of LLMs for automating requirements elicitation interviews, its novelty is somewhat limited by existing work in conversational RE and the small sample size of the evaluation. The limitations section of the paper reflects a solid self-awareness of these flaws. It presents a compelling argument for further investigation into the topic, but that is also its shortcoming.

### Reconstructing Close Human Interaction with Appearance and Proxemics Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02565v1)
- **Authors**: Buzhen Huang, Chen Li, Chongyang Xu, Dongyue Lu, Jinnan Chen, Yangang Wang, Gim Hee Lee
- **Abstract**: Due to visual ambiguities and inter-person occlusions, existing human pose estimation methods cannot recover plausible close interactions from in-the-wild videos. Even state-of-the-art large foundation models~(\eg, SAM) cannot accurately distinguish human semantics in such challenging scenarios. In this work, we find that human appearance can provide a straightforward cue to address these obstacles. Based on this observation, we propose a dual-branch optimization framework to reconstruct accurate interactive motions with plausible body contacts constrained by human appearances, social proxemics, and physical laws. Specifically, we first train a diffusion model to learn the human proxemic behavior and pose prior knowledge. The trained network and two optimizable tensors are then incorporated into a dual-branch optimization framework to reconstruct human motions and appearances. Several constraints based on 3D Gaussians, 2D keypoints, and mesh penetrations are also designed to assist the optimization. With the proxemics prior and diverse constraints, our method is capable of estimating accurate interactions from in-the-wild videos captured in complex environments. We further build a dataset with pseudo ground-truth interaction annotations, which may promote future research on pose estimation and human behavior understanding. Experimental results on several benchmarks demonstrate that our method outperforms existing approaches. The code and data are available at https://www.buzhenhuang.com/works/CloseApp.html.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on novelty, significance, and providing a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of reconstructing close human interactions from monocular in-the-wild videos, a challenging task due to visual ambiguities, inter-person occlusions, and the difficulty in establishing reliable body contacts and proxemic relationships. The core idea is to leverage human appearance as a crucial cue to resolve these ambiguities. The authors propose a dual-branch optimization framework:

1.  **Motion Branch:** Employs a diffusion model pre-trained on human interaction data (with a mask strategy to enhance robustness to occlusions) to learn a proxemic prior. This prior acts as a regularizer during optimization, guiding the motion reconstruction towards plausible interactions.
2.  **Appearance Branch:** Predicts 3D Gaussians to represent human appearance.  Two optimizable tensors are decoded to Gaussian UV maps, enabling differentiable rendering. This allows the framework to compare the rendered appearance with the original RGB image, providing a direct constraint on depth ordering and image-model alignment.
The framework incorporates various constraints, including 3D Gaussians, 2D keypoints, mesh penetration penalties, and physical laws.  The authors also contribute a new in-the-wild dataset (WildCHI) with pseudo ground-truth interaction annotations to facilitate research in this area.  Experimental results demonstrate that the method outperforms existing approaches and that the new dataset can improve the performance of other methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements:

    *   **Dual-Branch Optimization with Appearance Cue:** While optimization-based human reconstruction and proxemic priors are not entirely new, the explicit use of human appearance, modeled with 3D Gaussians, as a primary cue to resolve ambiguities in close interactions is a significant contribution. This differentiable rendering provides a strong, direct constraint not explored in previous works.
    *   **Proxemic Prior via Diffusion Model:** The use of a diffusion model to learn proxemic behavior from interactive data is a good approach. The mask strategy further makes the learned prior robust to occlusions which is necessary for these scenarios.
    *   **WildCHI Dataset:** The dataset addresses a critical gap in the availability of suitable data for this task. The effort to collect and annotate (even with pseudo ground-truth) in-the-wild interaction data is valuable.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important and challenging problem with many potential applications (robotics, VR/AR, surveillance).  The proposed approach shows promising results in improving the accuracy and plausibility of reconstructed interactions, particularly in cluttered and occluded scenes. The dataset is also a significant contribution, potentially enabling further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing methods and motivates the need for a new approach.
    *   **Well-Designed Framework:** The dual-branch framework is well-structured and leverages the strengths of both learned priors and direct image observations.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method and the value of the new dataset. Ablation studies clearly show the contribution of each component.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** Addresses the limited availability of suitable data for the task, potentially fostering further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Pseudo Ground Truth:** The use of pseudo ground truth in the WildCHI dataset is a potential limitation. While the authors claim to have filtered out incorrect estimations, the accuracy of the dataset is still limited. While understandable given the difficulty in obtaining real ground truth, this reliance on generated data limits the maximum possible impact of the dataset.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  Optimization-based methods are generally computationally expensive, which could limit their real-time applicability. The paper doesn't extensively discuss the computational performance.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The method is currently limited to two-person interactions. Scaling this approach to multi-person crowds would be a significant challenge.
    *  **Texture Quality Limitation:** The limitation of reconstructed textures under varying lighting conditions or occlusion. This is noted by the authors but is still a relevant constraint.

*   **Justification of Score:**
The paper presents a solid contribution to the field. The novelty, although building upon existing work, is clearly present in the effective use of differentiable rendering with 3D Gaussians and a new dataset. The results show clear improvements over existing methods. The limitations, primarily related to the pseudo ground truth and computational cost, are acknowledged but don't completely detract from the contribution. Overall, it is a well-executed piece of work that significantly improves the state-of-the-art in a challenging problem domain.
