### LLM-Driven Treatment Effect Estimation Under Inference Time Text Confounding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02843v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Ma, Dennis Frauen, Jonas Schweisthal, Stefan Feuerriegel
- **Abstract**: Estimating treatment effects is crucial for personalized decision-making in medicine, but this task faces unique challenges in clinical practice. At training time, models for estimating treatment effects are typically trained on well-structured medical datasets that contain detailed patient information. However, at inference time, predictions are often made using textual descriptions (e.g., descriptions with self-reported symptoms), which are incomplete representations of the original patient information. In this work, we make three contributions. (1) We show that the discrepancy between the data available during training time and inference time can lead to biased estimates of treatment effects. We formalize this issue as an inference time text confounding problem, where confounders are fully observed during training time but only partially available through text at inference time. (2) To address this problem, we propose a novel framework for estimating treatment effects that explicitly accounts for inference time text confounding. Our framework leverages large language models together with a custom doubly robust learner to mitigate biases caused by the inference time text confounding. (3) Through a series of experiments, we demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of biased treatment effect estimation when confounders are fully observed during training but only partially available through text descriptions at inference time (inference time text confounding).  The authors formalize this issue and propose a novel framework called TCA (Text Confounding Adjustment) to mitigate the bias. TCA leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate surrogates for the text confounders and incorporates a custom doubly robust learner for unbiased CATE estimates. The effectiveness of the framework is demonstrated through experiments on real-world medical datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in identifying and formalizing the problem of "inference time text confounding".  While existing literature addresses text as a confounder, it usually assumes the text is available in some form during training. The setting where *structured data* during training is only represented by *unstructured text* at inference is a realistic and under-explored scenario, particularly in applications like telemedicine. The proposed TCA framework provides a structured way to address this challenge, leveraging LLMs for text generation and doubly robust methods for CATE estimation.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the reliability and fairness of treatment effect predictions in healthcare settings. Biased treatment effect estimates can lead to suboptimal or even harmful treatment decisions.  By addressing the specific challenge of inference time text confounding, the paper contributes to more accurate personalized medicine. The experimental results appear to show a substantial improvement over naive methods, further highlighting the practical importance of the problem.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and formalization.
    *   Well-motivated framework combining LLMs and causal inference techniques.
    *   Empirical validation on relevant medical datasets (IST and MIMIC-III).
    *   Demonstrated improvement over naive baselines.
    * Doubly Robust learner ensures theoretical guarantees

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on LLMs introduces potential biases and concerns about data representation, which are acknowledged but could be explored in more detail.  The sensitivity of the framework to the specific LLM used and the prompt engineering techniques is worth considering.
    * The text generation mechanism is assumed to be independent of the noise variable in Assumption 3.2. A through assessment of this assumption and its impact on the results is missing.
    *  The reliance on semi-synthetic datasets makes it difficult to fully assess the real-world impact. While using real datasets, treatment, outcome, and confounders are generated to run experiments, hence a more realistic evaluation could be done.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research on causal inference, particularly in settings involving unstructured data and limited information at inference time.  It could also inspire the development of more robust and reliable methods for personalized medicine based on LLMs.  The clear articulation of the problem could lead to further research on this specific type of confounding.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to causal inference by identifying and addressing a novel and relevant problem. The proposed solution combines existing techniques (LLMs and doubly robust methods) in a creative way to overcome the challenges posed by inference time text confounding. The experimental results provide promising evidence of the framework's effectiveness. However, the limitations related to LLM biases, semi-synthetic datasets, and the strong theoretical assumptions slightly temper the assessment of the paper's overall impact. A higher degree of robustness analysis and real-world evaluation would boost confidence in the practicality and generalizability of this framework.

### Visual Contextual Attack: Jailbreaking MLLMs with Image-Driven Context Injection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02844v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqi Miao, Yi Ding, Lijun Li, Jing Shao
- **Abstract**: With the emergence of strong visual-language capabilities, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated tremendous potential for real-world applications. However, the security vulnerabilities exhibited by the visual modality pose significant challenges to deploying such models in open-world environments. Recent studies have successfully induced harmful responses from target MLLMs by encoding harmful textual semantics directly into visual inputs. However, in these approaches, the visual modality primarily serves as a trigger for unsafe behavior, often exhibiting semantic ambiguity and lacking grounding in realistic scenarios. In this work, we define a novel setting: visual-centric jailbreak, where visual information serves as a necessary component in constructing a complete and realistic jailbreak context. Building on this setting, we propose the VisCo (Visual Contextual) Attack. VisCo fabricates contextual dialogue using four distinct visual-focused strategies, dynamically generating auxiliary images when necessary to construct a visual-centric jailbreak scenario. To maximize attack effectiveness, it incorporates automatic toxicity obfuscation and semantic refinement to produce a final attack prompt that reliably triggers harmful responses from the target black-box MLLMs. Specifically, VisCo achieves a toxicity score of 4.78 and an Attack Success Rate (ASR) of 85% on MM-SafetyBench against GPT-4o, significantly outperforming the baseline, which performs a toxicity score of 2.48 and an ASR of 22.2%. The code is available at https://github.com/Dtc7w3PQ/Visco-Attack.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Visual Contextual Attack: Jailbreaking MLLMs with Image-Driven Context Injection."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack strategy, VisCo (Visual Contextual Attack), designed to jailbreak multimodal large language models (MLLMs). VisCo exploits the visual modality by crafting contextual dialogues that embed unsafe content, with visual information serving as a critical component of the jailbreak scenario. Unlike prior work where images merely act as triggers, VisCo focuses on creating realistic and visually coherent contexts by dynamically generating auxiliary images and employing various visual-focused strategies. The attack also incorporates automatic toxicity obfuscation and semantic refinement to improve its effectiveness in evading safety mechanisms. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of VisCo on multiple benchmarks, showing significant improvements in toxicity scores and attack success rates compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this work lies in its formal definition of "visual-centric jailbreak," a setting where visual information is not just a trigger but an essential ingredient for the jailbreak. The idea of using visual information to build a broader context around the attack is also interesting and moves beyond directly encoding harmful semantics in images.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights a serious vulnerability in MLLMs related to their ability to reason about and integrate visual information in a complex way. Showing that visually grounded adversarial scenarios can effectively bypass safety mechanisms raises important security concerns.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clearly defined problem setting and provides a well-structured solution.
    *   The VisCo attack is demonstrated to be effective across multiple MLLMs and benchmarks.
    *   The ablation study offers insights into the contribution of different components of the attack.
    *   The generated attack sequences are grounded in visually coherent scenarios, closely mimicking natural interactions between the user and the model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The use of predefined strategy templates for context fabrication limits the flexibility and scalability of the attack, restricting generalizability to new scenarios and prompts.
    *   The work relies on several auxiliary models, including vision-language, diffusion and large-language model (LLM) components, increasing the complexity of implementation.
    *   Ethical considerations could be explored more comprehensively, focusing on the balance between highlighting the risks and potentially providing blueprints for malicious actors.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on adversarial attacks and defense mechanisms for MLLMs. It underscores the importance of considering visual context when evaluating the security of these models and provides a framework for developing more robust defense strategies. The attack could spur new research directions for enhancing contextual understanding in MLLMs to prevent such exploitation of visual grounding capabilities.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The VisCo attack is technically well-executed and the problem setting is clearly defined and relevant. The results are convincing, and the ablation study provides useful insights. However, the dependence on predefined templates and auxiliary models somewhat limits the practical impact and generalizability. The idea of multi-stage prompts to elicit behaviors is well established, and the contribution of "visual centering" while novel, does not dramatically alter outcomes. It is also not clear if it generalizes beyond the datasets used. However, the paper presents a practical jailbreak that can be easily integrated with existing defense strategies (e.g., prompt filtering) and encourages future research.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MOTIF: Modular Thinking via Reinforcement Fine-tuning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02851v1)
- **Authors**: Purbesh Mitra, Sennur Ulukus
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) show that employing group relative policy optimization (GRPO) algorithm for reinforcement learning (RL) training allows the models to use more thinking/reasoning tokens for generating better responses. However, LLMs can generate only a finite amount of tokens while maintaining attention to the previously generated tokens. This limit, also known as the context size of an LLM, is a bottleneck in LLM reasoning with arbitrarily large number of tokens. To think beyond the limit of context size, an LLM must employ a modular thinking strategy to reason over multiple rounds. In this work, we propose $\textbf{MOTIF: Modular Thinking via Reinforcement Finetuning}$ -- an RL training method for generating thinking tokens in multiple rounds, effectively allowing the model to think with additional context size. We trained the open-source model Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct on GSM8K dataset via parameter efficient fine-tuning and tested its accuracy on MATH500 and AIME2024 benchmarks. Our experiments show 3.8\% and 3.3\% improvements over vanilla GRPO based training in the respective benchmarks. Furthermore, this improvement was achieved with only 15\% of samples, thus demonstrating sample efficiency of MOTIF. Our code and models are available at https://github.com/purbeshmitra/MOTIF and https://huggingface.co/purbeshmitra/MOTIF, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MOTIF, a reinforcement learning (RL) based method for fine-tuning Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve reasoning capabilities through modular thinking.  MOTIF trains LLMs to generate reasoning tokens in multiple rounds, effectively extending their context size beyond the standard limits. It employs a group relative policy optimization (GRPO) algorithm with an outcome-based reward function, focusing on the final answer accuracy without requiring supervision of intermediate reasoning steps. The method is evaluated on the GSM8K, MATH500, and AIME2024 benchmarks using the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct model, demonstrating improved accuracy compared to a vanilla GRPO baseline and increased sample efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's core contribution lies in the multi-round reinforcement learning framework tailored for LLMs, particularly the outcome-based reward function within the GRPO setup. While multi-round reasoning architectures are not entirely new (as acknowledged by the authors with references to INFTYTHINK and others), the use of *outcome-based* RL in this context distinguishes it.  The simplification of the reward function, focusing solely on the final answer, is a potential advantage and somewhat novel. It eliminates the need for complex process supervision, a common hurdle in RL for reasoning.  The paper's claim about sample efficiency is also significant if robustly validated.

*   **Significance:** Improving LLM reasoning is a very active and important area of research. Overcoming context size limitations is a fundamental challenge. MOTIF presents a practical approach to address this, demonstrating gains on challenging benchmarks. The method's focus on outcome-based rewards could make it more accessible and easier to implement than other RL-based approaches that require detailed reward engineering for intermediate reasoning steps. The reported sample efficiency, if it holds true across different models and tasks, would make it a valuable contribution to the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Demonstrated improvements on established benchmarks (MATH500, AIME2024).
    *   Use of outcome-based rewards simplifies the RL training process.
    *   Promising results regarding sample efficiency.
    *   The open-sourcing of code and models is a significant plus.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The gains over the GRPO baseline, while positive, are relatively modest. The impact on more complex datasets may be more relevant.
    *   The paper would benefit from more ablation studies and comparisons with other state-of-the-art multi-round reasoning methods, particularly those cited in the introduction.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a single model (Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct). Generalizability to other models needs to be shown.
    *   The claim about sample efficiency requires further investigation. Although the training uses fewer data points, the extra inference samples used during reward calculation impacts the overall computational resource consumption.

*   **Potential Influence:** MOTIF could influence research in several ways:

    *   By providing a practical and easy-to-implement RL framework for multi-round LLM reasoning.
    *   By highlighting the importance of outcome-based rewards in simplifying RL training.
    *   By encouraging further exploration of modular thinking strategies for LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.  While the paper doesn't revolutionize the field, it presents a solid, incremental improvement to existing techniques. The novelty lies primarily in the application of a simplified outcome-based RL framework within a multi-round reasoning architecture. The experimental results, while not groundbreaking, demonstrate tangible benefits. The promise of improved sample efficiency is noteworthy, but more rigorous validation is needed. The paper could have been strengthened by a more thorough comparison to other multi-round reasoning methods and by expanding the evaluation to include more diverse LLMs and datasets.  The open-sourcing of the code and models increases the paper's potential impact and promotes reproducibility.

### AnyI2V: Animating Any Conditional Image with Motion Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02857v1)
- **Authors**: Ziye Li, Hao Luo, Xincheng Shuai, Henghui Ding
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in video generation, particularly in diffusion models, have driven notable progress in text-to-video (T2V) and image-to-video (I2V) synthesis. However, challenges remain in effectively integrating dynamic motion signals and flexible spatial constraints. Existing T2V methods typically rely on text prompts, which inherently lack precise control over the spatial layout of generated content. In contrast, I2V methods are limited by their dependence on real images, which restricts the editability of the synthesized content. Although some methods incorporate ControlNet to introduce image-based conditioning, they often lack explicit motion control and require computationally expensive training. To address these limitations, we propose AnyI2V, a training-free framework that animates any conditional images with user-defined motion trajectories. AnyI2V supports a broader range of modalities as the conditional image, including data types such as meshes and point clouds that are not supported by ControlNet, enabling more flexible and versatile video generation. Additionally, it supports mixed conditional inputs and enables style transfer and editing via LoRA and text prompts. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed AnyI2V achieves superior performance and provides a new perspective in spatial- and motion-controlled video generation. Code is available at https://henghuiding.com/AnyI2V/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AnyI2V: Animating Any Conditional Image with Motion Control":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AnyI2V, a training-free framework designed to animate conditional images using user-defined motion trajectories. It addresses limitations in existing text-to-video (T2V) and image-to-video (I2V) methods regarding motion control, spatial constraints, and editability.  AnyI2V accepts diverse image modalities (mesh, point cloud, sketches, etc.) as input, supports mixed-modality conditioning, and facilitates content editing using LoRA or different text prompts. The core of the framework consists of three key components: structure-preserved feature injection, across-frames alignment, and semantic mask generation to ensure temporally coherent and spatially controlled video synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its training-free nature and its ability to handle a broad range of conditional image modalities beyond just RGB images or depth maps, effectively bridging the gap between I2V and T2V with significantly more flexibility. The integration of semantic masks for more precise motion control is also a notable contribution, mitigating the limitations of static masks used in other methods. While feature injection and alignment are present in other research areas, AnyI2V's implementation, with the de-biasing and semantic masking, provides a novel and effective solution.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to democratize video generation by removing the training barrier. The ability to use unconventional input modalities like meshes and point clouds makes it useful for diverse creative applications that are difficult with traditional techniques. The user-defined motion control facilitates fine-grained animation and editing, offering a new degree of artistic freedom.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-Free Approach:**  Eliminating the need for extensive training and making the model adaptable to different backbones is a considerable advantage.
    *   **Versatile Input Modalities:** The support for diverse and mixed input modalities expands the applicability of video generation.
    *   **Effective Motion Control:** The user-defined trajectory control combined with semantic masks offers precise control over object movement and shape deformation.
    *   **Strong Quantitative Results:** The ablation study demonstrates the positive impact of the paper's components on several metrics (FID, FVD, ObjMC).
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limitations in Motion Range and Occlusion Handling:** The paper acknowledges that AnyI2V struggles with large motion ranges and complex occlusions, which could limit its performance in certain scenarios.
    *   **Early Injection Limit:** The early denoising step injection may lose finer details that models like ControlNet can offer.
    *   **Dependency on Existing T2V Models:** AnyI2V builds on top of existing T2V backbones, inheriting some of their limitations.

*   **Impact:** AnyI2V has the potential to impact several areas:
    *   **Creative Content Creation:**  Enables artists and designers to animate and edit images from various sources and modalities with precise control.
    *   **Video Game Development:** Can be useful in creating animations for game characters or environments from existing 3D models.
    *   **Scientific Visualization:**  Can be used to animate scientific data, such as point clouds or mesh models, to visualize dynamic processes.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution with its training-free approach and versatile input support for video animation. The semantic mask strategy for motion control addresses a relevant limitation of current state-of-the-art techniques. While there are acknowledged limitations (motion range, early injection), the overall innovation and the potential to significantly broaden the scope of image animation techniques warrant a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Requirements Elicitation Follow-Up Question Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02858v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Shen, Anmol Singhal, Travis Breaux
- **Abstract**: Interviews are a widely used technique in eliciting requirements to gather stakeholder needs, preferences, and expectations for a software system. Effective interviewing requires skilled interviewers to formulate appropriate interview questions in real time while facing multiple challenges, including lack of familiarity with the domain, excessive cognitive load, and information overload that hinders how humans process stakeholders' speech. Recently, large language models (LLMs) have exhibited state-of-the-art performance in multiple natural language processing tasks, including text summarization and entailment. To support interviewers, we investigate the application of GPT-4o to generate follow-up interview questions during requirements elicitation by building on a framework of common interviewer mistake types. In addition, we describe methods to generate questions based on interviewee speech. We report a controlled experiment to evaluate LLM-generated and human-authored questions with minimal guidance, and a second controlled experiment to evaluate the LLM-generated questions when generation is guided by interviewer mistake types. Our findings demonstrate that, for both experiments, the LLM-generated questions are no worse than the human-authored questions with respect to clarity, relevancy, and informativeness. In addition, LLM-generated questions outperform human-authored questions when guided by common mistakes types. This highlights the potential of using LLMs to help interviewers improve the quality and ease of requirements elicitation interviews in real time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, to generate follow-up questions during requirements elicitation interviews. The authors aim to assist interviewers facing challenges like domain unfamiliarity and cognitive overload. They conduct several experiments: 1) analyze interview transcripts to determine the context needed for formulating questions; 2) compare minimally guided GPT-4o-generated questions with human-authored questions in terms of relevancy, clarity, and informativeness; 3) evaluate GPT-4o's ability to identify common interviewer mistakes; and 4) compare GPT-4o-generated questions (guided by mistake types to avoid) with human-authored questions. The findings suggest that GPT-4o performs comparably to humans in most cases, and even outperforms them when guided by specific interviewer mistake types. The authors argue that LLMs have the potential to enhance the quality and ease of requirements elicitation interviews.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of LLMs for *real-time* follow-up question generation during requirements elicitation interviews is a relatively novel contribution. Previous work primarily focused on interview preparation or post-processing. The analysis of interviewer mistakes and using those as guidance for LLM generation adds to the novelty. However, the core idea of using LLMs for text generation tasks is not new in itself.
*   **Significance:** Improving the efficiency and quality of requirements elicitation is crucial, as it directly impacts the success of software projects. By demonstrating the potential of LLMs to assist interviewers, the paper highlights a promising avenue for research and tool development. Reducing the burden on interviewers and potentially improving the quality of the elicited requirements has clear practical implications. However, the study primarily focuses on stakeholder *preferences*, which are one type of non-functional requirement; this specificity limits the generalizability of the study.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined research questions:** The paper clearly articulates the research questions and provides a systematic approach to answering them through empirical studies.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The use of controlled experiments, multiple quality metrics (relevancy, clarity, informativeness), and appropriate statistical tests (t-tests, mixed-effects models) strengthens the validity of the findings.
    *   **Analysis of interviewer mistakes:** The framework developed to categorize common interviewer mistakes is valuable and provides a solid foundation for guiding LLM generation.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The identified context window sizes (zero to four turns) for generating follow up questions has important practical implications for the design of LLM-based conversational agents for interviews.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to preference elicitation in directory service domains. The generalizability to other types of requirements and domains is unclear.
    *   **Human-authored question quality baseline:**  The quality of the 'human analyst-authored' questions might not reflect the best practices of expert interviewers and the question generation was not done in real-time, this may have influenced comparisons.
    *   **Subjectivity in evaluation:** The evaluation relies on human ratings of relevancy, clarity, and informativeness, which are inherently subjective. Blinding does help, but it doesn't eliminate the bias.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The authors claim "interviews should not be unethical or disrespectful" in the related works section, but it would strengthen the paper by clearly addressing ethical considerations for the use of an LLM assistant. For instance, stakeholders have a right to know when they're interacting with an LLM.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence research in the area of AI-assisted requirements engineering, particularly in the development of intelligent interviewing tools. It could also encourage further investigation into the cognitive factors affecting interviewer performance and how LLMs can mitigate these limitations. The mistake framework could provide researchers with concrete concepts for improving and evaluating LLM agents used in elicitation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a solid, well-executed study with interesting and potentially impactful findings. The systematic methodology, the analysis of interviewer mistakes, and the comparison with human-authored questions are valuable contributions. However, the limited scope, subjectivity in evaluation, and dependence on LLM performance place constraints on its immediate impact. The ethical discussion is limited to a statement in the related works.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Bootstrapping Grounded Chain-of-Thought in Multimodal LLMs for Data-Efficient Model Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02859v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaer Xia, Bingkui Tong, Yuhang Zang, Rui Shao, Kaiyang Zhou
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in interpreting images using natural language. However, without using large-scale datasets for retraining, these models are difficult to adapt to specialized vision tasks, e.g., chart understanding. This problem is caused by a mismatch between pre-training and downstream datasets: pre-training datasets primarily concentrate on scenes and objects but contain limited information about specialized, non-object images, such as charts and tables. In this paper, we share an interesting finding that training an MLLM with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning data can facilitate model adaptation in specialized vision tasks, especially under data-limited regimes. However, we identify a critical issue within CoT data distilled from pre-trained MLLMs, i.e., the data often contains multiple factual errors in the reasoning steps. To address the problem, we propose Grounded Chain-of-Thought (GCoT), a simple bootstrapping-based approach that aims to inject grounding information (i.e., bounding boxes) into CoT data, essentially making the reasoning steps more faithful to input images. We evaluate our approach on five specialized vision tasks, which cover a variety of visual formats including charts, tables, receipts, and reports. The results demonstrate that under data-limited regimes our approach significantly improves upon fine-tuning and distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Bootstrapping Grounded Chain-of-Thought in Multimodal LLMs for Data-Efficient Model Adaptation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of adapting Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to specialized vision tasks, particularly those involving non-object-centric images like charts and tables, under data-limited scenarios.  The authors observe that directly fine-tuning or distilling with regular Chain-of-Thought (CoT) data can be limited due to factual inaccuracies in the reasoning steps generated by pre-trained MLLMs.  They propose Grounded Chain-of-Thought (GCoT), a bootstrapping approach.  GCoT involves using a pre-trained MLLM for visual grounding (object localization) to generate bounding boxes for key elements in the CoT reasoning steps. A self-verification process then validates the extracted information within those bounding boxes. The resulting grounded and validated CoT data is used to fine-tune the MLLM.  The authors demonstrate that GCoT significantly improves performance on five specialized vision tasks compared to zero-shot, fine-tuning, and regular CoT distillation baselines, especially under data-scarce conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of grounding CoT data with visual cues (bounding boxes) to improve factual accuracy in reasoning is a valuable contribution. It directly addresses the limitations of using pre-trained MLLMs on specialized tasks where object-centric knowledge isn't sufficient. The bootstrapping approach for generating the grounded CoT data itself is novel, as is the use of self-verification using localization to minimize annotation effort. The concept of grounding CoT with bounding boxes is somewhat explored in the community but this work provides a solid end-to-end framework.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a practical problem: adapting MLLMs to new visual formats when large-scale, task-specific training data is unavailable or expensive to create. The reported performance gains are significant, especially in the few-shot regime, making it a valuable approach for real-world applications. The results provide a compelling demonstration of CoT data in facilitating model adaptation. The ability to ground data helps to reduce errors in reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies the mismatch between pre-training and downstream datasets as a key bottleneck in adapting MLLMs.
    *   **Effective Solution:** GCoT is a technically sound and practically effective method for improving adaptation.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Thorough experiments on five diverse datasets demonstrate the robustness of the approach. The ablation studies are insightful.
    *   **Data Efficiency:** The paper highlights the data efficiency of GCoT, which is crucial for many real-world scenarios.
    *   The findings showcase strong performance over alternative approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The approach still relies on a pre-trained MLLM for visual grounding, which itself needs to be trained on some relevant data, potentially requiring a multi-stage training pipeline. The approach also depends on distilling from a 3rd party MLLM.
    *   **Limited Applicability:** While the paper demonstrates effectiveness on charts, tables, receipts, and reports, the visual grounding approach may not be directly applicable to all vision tasks, particularly those without clearly definable objects.
    *   **Complexity:** The bootstrapping process adds complexity compared to simpler fine-tuning or distillation approaches.
    *   **Potential for cascading errors:** As the bounding boxes are generated and evaluated, errors are likely to occur in that process which may propagate to the end result and ultimately limit accuracy.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:

    *   **MLLM Adaptation:**  Provides a valuable method for adapting MLLMs to specialized vision tasks.
    *   **Grounded Reasoning:** Encourages further exploration of grounding techniques for improving reasoning in multimodal models.
    *   **Data-Efficient Learning:** Showcases the benefits of using reasoning to achieve better generalization with limited data.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective technique for adapting MLLMs to specialized vision tasks under data-constrained settings. The self-verification of GCoT data is unique and significantly contributes to high-quality data which benefits few shot performance. While there are limitations in terms of reliance on pre-trained models and applicability to certain tasks, the significant performance gains, clear problem definition, and thorough experimental validation make it a valuable contribution to the field. Therefore, while not a groundbreaking paradigm shift, it presents a substantial improvement on existing methods and provides a solid foundation for future research.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Less is Enough: Training-Free Video Diffusion Acceleration via Runtime-Adaptive Caching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02860v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Zhou, Dingkang Liang, Kaijin Chen, Tianrui Feng, Xiwu Chen, Hongkai Lin, Yikang Ding, Feiyang Tan, Hengshuang Zhao, Xiang Bai
- **Abstract**: Video generation models have demonstrated remarkable performance, yet their broader adoption remains constrained by slow inference speeds and substantial computational costs, primarily due to the iterative nature of the denoising process. Addressing this bottleneck is essential for democratizing advanced video synthesis technologies and enabling their integration into real-world applications. This work proposes EasyCache, a training-free acceleration framework for video diffusion models. EasyCache introduces a lightweight, runtime-adaptive caching mechanism that dynamically reuses previously computed transformation vectors, avoiding redundant computations during inference. Unlike prior approaches, EasyCache requires no offline profiling, pre-computation, or extensive parameter tuning. We conduct comprehensive studies on various large-scale video generation models, including OpenSora, Wan2.1, and HunyuanVideo. Our method achieves leading acceleration performance, reducing inference time by up to 2.1-3.3$\times$ compared to the original baselines while maintaining high visual fidelity with a significant up to 36% PSNR improvement compared to the previous SOTA method. This improvement makes our EasyCache a efficient and highly accessible solution for high-quality video generation in both research and practical applications. The code is available at https://github.com/H-EmbodVis/EasyCache.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EasyCache, a training-free acceleration framework for video diffusion models. EasyCache leverages a runtime-adaptive caching mechanism to reuse previously computed transformation vectors, thereby avoiding redundant computations during inference.  Unlike prior approaches, EasyCache doesn't require offline profiling, pre-computation, or extensive parameter tuning. The method achieves significant speedups on models like OpenSora, Wan2.1, and Hunyuan Video while maintaining high visual fidelity, even improving PSNR compared to existing state-of-the-art (SOTA) caching methods. The paper identifies and exploits the relative stability of transformation rates in Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) as the basis for its adaptive caching strategy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in its truly runtime-adaptive caching strategy.  Prior caching methods, including TeaCache, relied on offline profiling or fixed heuristics, which limits generalizability. EasyCache's online approach, based on monitoring the relative transformation rate, is a significant step forward. The insight that transformation rates stabilize during the denoising process is a crucial contribution. However, the derivative estimation approach is relatively rudimentary. Other methods may use more complex or precise methods to estimate derivatives. EasyCache is also able to be used together with other acceleration techniques (Efficient Attention) adding to its strength.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of EasyCache is high. It addresses a major bottleneck in diffusion models: slow inference speeds and high computational costs. Democratizing high-quality video generation by making it more accessible to resource-constrained environments is valuable. The speedups achieved are substantial, and the maintenance (or even improvement) of visual fidelity compared to other accelerated methods makes EasyCache attractive.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free:**  The absence of any training or pre-computation requirements is a major advantage for widespread adoption.
    *   **Runtime-adaptive:** The core contribution in adaptability to changing inference conditions. This is significantly better compared to static or pre-calculated policies.
    *   **Compatibility:** EasyCache is orthogonal to existing methods. It can be combined with Efficient Attention to boost acceleration even further.
    *   **Performance:** EasyCache demonstrably outperforms state-of-the-art caching methods like TeaCache in both speed and visual quality.
    *   **Clear presentation and evaluation:** The paper is well-written with clear results and comprehensive experiments across various video generation models.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplicity of the approach:** While the runtime-adaptive criterion is novel, the underlying mechanisms of transformation rate estimation are relatively simple (L1 norm). It does not explore more complex or refined methods.

    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** There remains hyperparameter tuning. In particular, the threshold τ remains a relevant parameter that affects the effectiveness of the algorithm.

    *   **VBench Sensitivity:** There are indications that EasyCache slightly lowers performance compared to other state-of-the-art in perceptual metrics and that it is sensitive to structural variations.

**Potential Influence:**

EasyCache has the potential to become a widely adopted technique in the video generation community.  Its simplicity, effectiveness, and broad applicability make it a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners alike. It also creates an incentive to explore deeper into analyzing transformation rate stabilization in video diffusion models.

**Overall Assessment:**

EasyCache represents a significant advancement in training-free video diffusion acceleration.  While the underlying mechanisms are relatively simple, the insights and engineering behind its runtime-adaptive approach are compelling.  The performance gains and practical benefits are substantial. The simplicity of EasyCache makes it valuable despite any shortcomings.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a clear advance in the field of training-free video diffusion acceleration. The adaptive caching mechanism, by relying on dynamic transformation-rate measurements, provides significant improvements compared to previous static methods. The results are rigorously tested, and the performance is convincingly superior, however there is still an element of sensitivity to certain metrics, and other methods might have better performance at these metrics. It would be better if the paper explores more complex or refine methods for analyzing dynamics of internal features. The improvement also shows the paper is practically useful to the diffusion model community.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10
